You can log in using one of the pre-configured user accounts below to explore the features available to different roles.\n8\t\n9\t### Login Credentials\n10\t\n11\tAll users share the same password for this demonstration.\n12\t\n13\t**Password:** `password123`\n14\t\n15\t| Name             | Email                  | Role              | Tenant      | Status    |\n16\t| ---------------- | ---------------------- | ----------------- | ----------- | --------- |\n17\t| Super Admin      | `super@omni.ai`        | Super Admin       | Platform    | Active    |\n18\t| Alice Admin      | `alice@acme.com`       | Tenant Admin      | Acme Corp   | Active    |\n19\t| Bob Security     | `bob@acme.com`         | SecOps Analyst    | Acme Corp   | Active    |\n20\t| Charlie DevOps   | `charlie@acme.com`     | DevOps Engineer   | *Disabled*  |\n21\t| Eve Engineer     | `eve@initech.com`      | Tenant Admin      | Initech     | Active    |\n22\t\n23\t### Self-Service Registration\n24\t\n25\tYou can also register a new company (tenant) and a corresponding administrator account directly from the login screen.\n26\t\n27\t1.  Navigate to the login page.\n28\t2.  Click on \"Sign Up\".\n29\t3.  Fill in your company name, your name, email, and a password.\n30\t4.  Upon successful registration, you will be automatically logged in as the \"Tenant Admin\" for your new workspace.\n31\t\n32\t## Key Features\n33\t\n34\tThe platform is a comprehensive, multi-tenant solution designed to provide a single pane of glass for modern IT operations, security, and governance.\n35\t\n36\t### Core Platform & UI/UX\n37\t\n38\t*   **Multi-Tenancy Architecture:** Full data segregation between customer tenants, with a Super Admin role for platform-wide management.\n39\t*   **Authentication & Authorization:** Secure login system with self-service workspace registration and a granular Role-Based Access Control (RBAC) engine.\n40\t*   **Interactive Guided Tour:** An animated AI character, \"Omni,\" provides a step-by-step tour of the platform's features and navigates users between pages.\n41\t*   **Customizable UI:** Features a full Light/Dark mode and a collapsible sidebar with organized, expandable sections for easy navigation.\n42\t*   **CXOs Dashboard:** An exclusive, high-level dashboard for executives with multiple switchable themes (Focus, Matrix, Executive, Glass, Strategic) to present strategic KPIs.\n43\t*   **Real-time Notifications:** A centralized notification system keeps users informed of important events.\n44\t*   **Local Persistence:** Uses browser `localStorage` to simulate a database, ensuring all data and configurations persist across page reloads.\n45\t\n46\t### AI-Powered Autonomous Operations\n47\t\n48\t*   **AI Command Bar (`Cmd+K`):** Use natural language to navigate the app, apply filters, and execute commands.\n49\t*   **Context-Aware AI Chat Assistant:** A floating chat assistant that has context on the current dashboard, allowing users to ask questions about the on-screen data.\n50\t*   **AI-Driven Analysis:** AI-generated insights and summaries are available on dashboards for Health, Security, and FinOps, correlating metrics and events to provide actionable recommendations.\n51\t*   **Autonomous Remediation:** The platform can autonomously diagnose and remediate agent errors, CSPM findings, and AI system risks, with detailed agentic step-by-step logging.\n52\t*   **AI-Generated SOAR Playbooks:** Generate security automation playbooks from a natural language prompt.\n53\t*   **AI-Powered Code Correction:** Automatically generate secure code fixes for vulnerabilities found during Static Application Security Testing (SAST).\n54\t\n55\t### Unified Observability\n56\t\n57\t*   **Agent Fleet Management:** A central dashboard to monitor, manage, and install agents across various platforms (Linux, Windows, Kubernetes). Includes remote actions, capability management, and a version upgrade manager.\n58\t*   **Asset Management:** A complete inventory of all hardware and software assets, with detailed views, vulnerability scanning, and File Integrity Monitoring (FIM).\n59\t*   **Log Explorer:** A centralized interface to search, filter, and analyze logs from all services and agents.\n60\t*   **Distributed Tracing:** Visualize request flows and service dependencies in a microservices architecture to identify performance bottlenecks.\n61\t*   **Network Observability:** Agentless monitoring for network devices (routers, switches, firewalls) with configuration change tracking and vulnerability detection.\n62\t*   **Proactive Insights:** AI-driven dashboard for predictive alerts, anomaly detection, and automated Root Cause Analysis (RCA).\n63\t\n64\t### Comprehensive Security (XDR, SIEM & SOAR)\n65\t\n66\t*   **Security Operations Center (SOC):** A unified dashboard for SIEM and SOAR, featuring a live security event feed, case management, and playbook automation.\n67\t*   **Threat Hunting (UEBA):** Proactively identifies insider threats and compromised accounts using User and Entity Behavior Analytics.\n68\t*   **Cloud Security Posture Management (CSPM):** Continuously monitor cloud accounts (AWS, Azure, GCP) for misconfigurations and security risks, with AI-generated remediation guides.\n69\t*   **Data Security Posture Management (DSPM):** Discover, classify, and protect sensitive data (PII, Financial, IP) across all enterprise assets.\n70\t*   **Attack Path Analysis:** Visualize potential adversary paths from points of entry to critical \"crown jewel\" assets.\n71\t*   **Patch Management:** A dedicated dashboard to monitor and deploy security patches across all managed assets, with job scheduling and tracking.\n72\t*   **Live Threat Intelligence:** Integrates with threat intelligence feeds (like VirusTotal) to scan artifacts (IPs, domains, hashes) in real-time.\n73\t*   **Incident Impact Analysis:** Automatically generates a \"blast radius\" graph to visualize the potential business and service impact of a security alert or case.\n74\t\n75\t### DevSecOps & Platform Engineering\n76\t\n77\t*   **DevSecOps Dashboard:** A \"shift-left\" security dashboard that provides insights from SAST and Software Bill of Materials (SBOM) analysis.\n78\t*   **Static Application Security Testing (SAST):** Scans code repositories for vulnerabilities and provides AI-powered code fixes.\n79\t*   **Software Supply Chain Security (SBOM):** Upload and analyze SBOM files to identify components and their associated vulnerabilities.\n80\t*   **DORA Metrics:** Track key DevOps performance indicators: Deployment Frequency, Lead Time for Changes, Change Failure Rate, and Mean Time to Recovery.\n81\t*   **Service Catalog (IDP):** An Internal Developer Platform where developers can provision new services using approved, secure-by-default templates.\n82\t*   **Chaos Engineering:** Proactively test system resilience by running controlled failure experiments (e.g., CPU Hog, Latency Injection).\n83\t*   **Developer Hub:** Centralized API documentation for all platform services to enable custom integrations.\n84\t\n85\t### Governance, Risk, and Compliance (GRC)\n86\t\n87\t*   **Compliance Management:** Track and manage compliance against major frameworks like SOC 2, ISO 27001, GDPR, HIPAA, PCI DSS, and NIST CSF.\n88\t*   **AI Governance (ISO 42001):** A dedicated dashboard to manage the entire AI lifecycle, including an AI System Registry, impact assessments, fairness metrics, risk management, and a model registry.\n89\t*   **Audit Log:** An immutable, searchable log of all significant user and system actions for security and compliance audits.\n90\t*   **Automation Policies:** Define rules for when and how the platform can take autonomous actions, ensuring human-in-the-loop control.\n91\t\n92\t### Administration & Management\n93\t\n94\t*   **Tenant Management:** A Super Admin dashboard to oversee all customer tenants, manage subscriptions, and monitor platform-wide resource usage.\n95\t*   **Settings Dashboard:** A centralized hub for all configurations, including:\n96\t    *   **User Management & RBAC:** Create, edit, and disable users; create and manage custom roles with granular permissions.\n97\t    *   **API Key Management:** Generate and revoke API keys for programmatic access.\n98\t    *   **Integrations Marketplace:** Configure third-party integrations (e.g., Slack, Jira, PagerDuty).\n99\t    *   **Subscription Management:** Admins can manage the features enabled for their tenant.\n100\t*   **FinOps & Billing:** Monitor cloud resource consumption and costs with AI-powered analysis and cost-saving recommendations."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"7c035973-35b1-4875-9c68-618c8dc0f461","uuid":"cd2d74c0-e2e5-40b3-aed9-436084e33d11","timestamp":"2026-08-21T21:42:00.108Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/README.md","content":"# Enterprise Omni-Agent AI Platform\n\nA production-ready, enterprise-grade autonomous observability and AI governance platform that combines intelligent monitoring with responsible AI management, featuring real-time dashboards, AI-driven insights, and comprehensive compliance tracking.\n\n## Getting Started\n\nThis application is a demonstration of a multi-tenant enterprise platform. You can log in using one of the pre-configured user accounts below to explore the features available to different roles.\n\n### Login Credentials\n\nAll users share the same password for this demonstration.\n\n**Password:** `password123`\n\n| Name             | Email                  | Role              | Tenant      | Status    |\n| ---------------- | ---------------------- | ----------------- | ----------- | --------- |\n| Super Admin      | `super@omni.ai`        | Super Admin       | Platform    | Active    |\n| Alice Admin      | `alice@acme.com`       | Tenant Admin      | Acme Corp   | Active    |\n| Bob Security     | `bob@acme.com`         | SecOps Analyst    | Acme Corp   | Active    |\n| Charlie DevOps   | `charlie@acme.com`     | DevOps Engineer   | *Disabled*  |\n| Eve Engineer     | `eve@initech.com`      | Tenant Admin      | Initech     | Active    |\n\n### Self-Service Registration\n\nYou can also register a new company (tenant) and a corresponding administrator account directly from the login screen.\n\n1.  Navigate to the login page.\n2.  Click on \"Sign Up\".\n3.  Fill in your company name, your name, email, and a password.\n4.  Upon successful registration, you will be automatically logged in as the \"Tenant Admin\" for your new workspace.\n\n## Key Features\n\nThe platform is a comprehensive, multi-tenant solution designed to provide a single pane of glass for modern IT operations, security, and governance.\n\n### Core Platform & UI/UX\n\n*   **Multi-Tenancy Architecture:** Full data segregation between customer tenants, with a Super Admin role for platform-wide management.\n*   **Authentication & Authorization:** Secure login system with self-service workspace registration and a granular Role-Based Access Control (RBAC) engine.\n*   **Interactive Guided Tour:** An animated AI character, \"Omni,\" provides a step-by-step tour of the platform's features and navigates users between pages.\n*   **Customizable UI:** Features a full Light/Dark mode and a collapsible sidebar with organized, expandable sections for easy navigation.\n*   **CXOs Dashboard:** An exclusive, high-level dashboard for executives with multiple switchable themes (Focus, Matrix, Executive, Glass, Strategic) to present strategic KPIs.\n*   **Real-time Notifications:** A centralized notification system keeps users informed of important events.\n*   **Local Persistence:** Uses browser `localStorage` to simulate a database, ensuring all data and configurations persist across page reloads.\n\n### AI-Powered Autonomous Operations\n\n*   **AI Command Bar (`Cmd+K`):** Use natural language to navigate the app, apply filters, and execute commands.\n*   **Context-Aware AI Chat Assistant:** A floating chat assistant that has context on the current dashboard, allowing users to ask questions about the on-screen data.\n*   **AI-Driven Analysis:** AI-generated insights and summaries are available on dashboards for Health, Security, and FinOps, correlating metrics and events to provide actionable recommendations.\n*   **Autonomous Remediation:** The platform can autonomously diagnose and remediate agent errors, CSPM findings, and AI system risks, with detailed agentic step-by-step logging.\n*   **AI-Generated SOAR Playbooks:** Generate security automation playbooks from a natural language prompt.\n*   **AI-Powered Code Correction:** Automatically generate secure code fixes for vulnerabilities found during Static Application Security Testing (SAST).\n\n### Unified Observability\n\n*   **Agent Fleet Management:** A central dashboard to monitor, manage, and install agents across various platforms (Linux, Windows, Kubernetes). Includes remote actions, capability management, and a version upgrade manager.\n*   **Asset Management:** A complete inventory of all hardware and software assets, with detailed views, vulnerability scanning, and File Integrity Monitoring (FIM).\n*   **Log Explorer:** A centralized interface to search, filter, and analyze logs from all services and agents.\n*   **Distributed Tracing:** Visualize request flows and service dependencies in a microservices architecture to identify performance bottlenecks.\n*   **Network Observability:** Agentless monitoring for network devices (routers, switches, firewalls) with configuration change tracking and vulnerability detection.\n*   **Proactive Insights:** AI-driven dashboard for predictive alerts, anomaly detection, and automated Root Cause Analysis (RCA).\n\n### Comprehensive Security (XDR, SIEM & SOAR)\n\n*   **Security Operations Center (SOC):** A unified dashboard for SIEM and SOAR, featuring a live security event feed, case management, and playbook automation.\n*   **Threat Hunting (UEBA):** Proactively identifies insider threats and compromised accounts using User and Entity Behavior Analytics.\n*   **Cloud Security Posture Management (CSPM):** Continuously monitor cloud accounts (AWS, Azure, GCP) for misconfigurations and security risks, with AI-generated remediation guides.\n*   **Data Security Posture Management (DSPM):** Discover, classify, and protect sensitive data (PII, Financial, IP) across all enterprise assets.\n*   **Attack Path Analysis:** Visualize potential adversary paths from points of entry to critical \"crown jewel\" assets.\n*   **Patch Management:** A dedicated dashboard to monitor and deploy security patches across all managed assets, with job scheduling and tracking.\n*   **Live Threat Intelligence:** Integrates with threat intelligence feeds (like VirusTotal) to scan artifacts (IPs, domains, hashes) in real-time.\n*   **Incident Impact Analysis:** Automatically generates a \"blast radius\" graph to visualize the potential business and service impact of a security alert or case.\n\n### DevSecOps & Platform Engineering\n\n*   **DevSecOps Dashboard:** A \"shift-left\" security dashboard that provides insights from SAST and Software Bill of Materials (SBOM) analysis.\n*   **Static Application Security Testing (SAST):** Scans code repositories for vulnerabilities and provides AI-powered code fixes.\n*   **Software Supply Chain Security (SBOM):** Upload and analyze SBOM files to identify components and their associated vulnerabilities.\n*   **DORA Metrics:** Track key DevOps performance indicators: Deployment Frequency, Lead Time for Changes, Change Failure Rate, and Mean Time to Recovery.\n*   **Service Catalog (IDP):** An Internal Developer Platform where developers can provision new services using approved, secure-by-default templates.\n*   **Chaos Engineering:** Proactively test system resilience by running controlled failure experiments (e.g., CPU Hog, Latency Injection).\n*   **Developer Hub:** Centralized API documentation for all platform services to enable custom integrations.\n\n### Governance, Risk, and Compliance (GRC)\n\n*   **Compliance Management:** Track and manage compliance against major frameworks like SOC 2, ISO 27001, GDPR, HIPAA, PCI DSS, and NIST CSF.\n*   **AI Governance (ISO 42001):** A dedicated dashboard to manage the entire AI lifecycle, including an AI System Registry, impact assessments, fairness metrics, risk management, and a model registry.\n*   **Audit Log:** An immutable, searchable log of all significant user and system actions for security and compliance audits.\n*   **Automation Policies:** Define rules for when and how the platform can take autonomous actions, ensuring human-in-the-loop control.\n\n### Administration & Management\n\n*   **Tenant Management:** A Super Admin dashboard to oversee all customer tenants, manage subscriptions, and monitor platform-wide resource usage.\n*   **Settings Dashboard:** A centralized hub for all configurations, including:\n    *   **User Management & RBAC:** Create, edit, and disable users; create and manage custom roles with granular permissions.\n    *   **API Key Management:** Generate and revoke API keys for programmatic access.\n    *   **Integrations Marketplace:** Configure third-party integrations (e.g., Slack, Jira, PagerDuty).\n    *   **Subscription Management:** Admins can manage the features enabled for their tenant.\n*   **FinOps & Billing:** Monitor cloud resource consumption and costs with AI-powered analysis and cost-saving recommendations.","numLines":100,"startLine":1,"totalLines":111}}},{"type":"system","subtype":"thinking_tokens","estimated_tokens":1,"estimated_tokens_delta":1,"uuid":"dac0f62e-7013-4eea-81f3-20399339cfd2","session_id":"7c035973-35b1-4875-9c68-618c8dc0f461"},{"type":"system","subtype":"thinking_tokens","estimated_tokens":4,"estimated_tokens_delta":3,"uuid":"c80e71b9-7928-4f39-a197-9e03f0ed0b61","session_id":"7c035973-35b1-4875-9c68-618c8dc0f461"},{"type":"system","subtype":"thinking_tokens","estimated_tokens":7,"estimated_tokens_delta":3,"uuid":"72474991-db47-4df7-a238-20e7641a78a8","session_id":"7c035973-35b1-4875-9c68-618c8dc0f461"},{"type":"system","subtype":"thinking_tokens","estimated_tokens":14,"estimated_tokens_delta":7,"uuid":"5003f217-e4ca-4927-b67e-5486dc6f67b0","session_id":"7c035973-35b1-4875-9c68-618c8dc0f461"},{"type":"system","subtype":"thinking_tokens","estimated_tokens":20,"estimated_tokens_delta":6,"uuid":"17265e78-ab6c-4447-b498-9f3ed4d8cb16","session_id":"7c035973-35b1-4875-9c68-618c8dc0f461"},{"type":"assistant","message":{"id":"f6f85bc2-02f8-4083-9aca-d158de6eaea5","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"nvidia/nemotron-3-ultra-550b-a55b","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Let Spawned by /gsd-plan-phase orchestrator.\n4\ttools: Read, Write, Edit, Bash, Glob, Grep, Skill, WebFetch, mcp__context7__*, mcp__plugin_context7_context7__*\n5\tcolor: green\n6\t# hooks:\n7\t#   PostToolUse:\n8\t#     - matcher: \"Write|Edit\"\n9\t#       hooks:\n10\t#         - type: command\n11\t#           command: \"npx eslint --fix $FILE 2>/dev/null || true\"\n12\teffort: xhigh\n13\t---\n14\t\n15\t<role>\n16\tYou are a GSD planner. You create executable phase plans with task breakdown, dependency analysis, and goal-backward verification.\n17\t\n18\tSpawned by:\n19\t- `/gsd-plan-phase` orchestrator (standard phase planning)\n20\t- `/gsd-plan-phase --gaps` orchestrator (gap closure from verification failures)\n21\t- `/gsd-plan-phase` in revision mode (updating plans based on checker feedback)\n22\t- `/gsd-plan-phase --reviews` orchestrator (replanning with cross-AI review feedback)\n23\t\n24\tYour job: Produce PLAN.md files that Claude executors can implement without interpretation. Plans are prompts, not documents that become prompts.\n25\t\n26\t@/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/mandatory-initial-read.md\n27\t\n28\t**Core responsibilities:**\n29\t- **FIRST: Parse and honor user decisions from CONTEXT.md** (locked decisions are NON-NEGOTIABLE)\n30\t- Decompose phases into parallel-optimized plans with 2-3 tasks each\n31\t- Build dependency graphs and assign execution waves\n32\t- Derive must-haves using goal-backward methodology\n33\t- Handle both standard planning and gap closure mode\n34\t- Revise existing plans based on checker feedback (revision mode)\n35\t- Return structured results to orchestrator\n36\t</role>\n37\t\n38\t<documentation_lookup>\n39\tFor library docs: prefer Context7 MCP. If unavailable, use `command -v ctx7` then `ctx7 library <name> \"<query>\"` and `ctx7 docs <libraryId> \"<query>\"`. Never use `npx --yes ctx7@latest`.\n40\t</documentation_lookup>\n41\t\n42\t<project_context>\n43\tBefore planning, discover project context:\n44\t\n45\t**Project instructions:** Read `./CLAUDE.md` if it exists in the working directory. Follow all project-specific guidelines, security requirements, and coding conventions.\n46\t\n47\t**Project skills:** @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/project-skills-discovery.md\n48\t- Load `rules/*.md` as needed during **planning**.\n49\t- Ensure plans account for project skill patterns and conventions.\n50\t\n51\t**agent_skills:** self-load per @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/agent-skills-bootstrap.md\n52\t</project_context>\n53\t\n54\t<context_fidelity>\n55\t## CRITICAL: User Decision Fidelity\n56\t\n57\tThe orchestrator provides user decisions in `<user_decisions>` tags from `/gsd-discuss-phase`.\n58\t\n59\t**Before creating ANY task, verify:**\n60\t\n61\t1. **Locked Decisions (from `## Decisions`)** — MUST be implemented exactly as specified. Reference the decision ID (D-01, D-02, etc.) in task actions for traceability.\n62\t\n63\t2. **Deferred Ideas (from `## Deferred Ideas`)** — MUST NOT appear in plans.\n64\t\n65\t3. **Claude's Discretion (from `## Claude's Discretion`)** — Use your judgment; document choices in task actions.\n66\t\n67\t**Self-check before returning:** For each plan, verify:\n68\t- [ ] Every locked decision (D-01, D-02, etc.) has a task implementing it\n69\t- [ ] Task actions reference the decision ID they implement (e.g., \"per D-03\")\n70\t      (The decision-coverage gate `check.decision-coverage-plan` reads D-NN citations from `<objective>`, `<tasks>`, `<task>`, `<action>`, `<read_first>`, `<behavior>`, `<verify>`, `<acceptance_criteria>`, and `<done>` tag bodies, as well as `## must_haves`/`truths`/`tasks`/`objective` markdown headings and front-matter `must_haves`/`truths`/`objective` keys — citing D-NN in any of these locations counts toward coverage.)\n71\t- [ ] No task implements a deferred idea\n72\t- [ ] Discretion areas are handled reasonably\n73\t\n74\t**If conflict exists** (e.g., research suggests library Y but user locked library X):\n75\t- Honor the user's locked decision\n76\t- Note in task action: \"Using X per user decision (research suggested Y)\"\n77\t</context_fidelity>\n78\t\n79\t<scope_reduction_prohibition>\n80\t## CRITICAL: Never Simplify User Decisions — Split Instead\n81\t\n82\t**PROHIBITED language/patterns in task actions:**\n83\t- \"v1\", \"v2\", \"simplified version\", \"static for now\", \"hardcoded for now\"\n84\t- \"future enhancement\", \"placeholder\", \"basic version\", \"minimal implementation\"\n85\t- \"will be wired later\", \"dynamic in future phase\", \"skip for now\"\n86\t- Any language that reduces a source artifact decision to less than what was specified\n87\t\n88\t**The rule:** If D-XX says \"display cost calculated from billing table in impulses\", the plan MUST deliver cost calculated from billing table in impulses. NOT \"static label /min\" as a \"v1\".\n89\t\n90\t**When the plan set cannot cover all source items within context budget:**\n91\t\n92\tDo NOT silently omit features. Instead:\n93\t\n94\t1. **Create a multi-source coverage audit** (see below) covering ALL four artifact types\n95\t2. **If any item cannot fit** within the plan budget (context cost exceeds capacity):\n96\t   - Return `## PHASE SPLIT RECOMMENDED` to the orchestrator\n97\t   - Propose how to split: which item groups form natural sub-phases\n98\t3. The orchestrator presents the split to the user for approval\n99\t4. After approval, plan each sub-phase within budget\n100\t\n101\t## Multi-Source Coverage Audit (MANDATORY in every plan set)\n102\t\n103\t@/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-source-audit.md for full format, examples, and gap-handling rules.\n104\t\n105\tAudit ALL four source types before finalizing: **GOAL** (ROADMAP phase goal), **REQ** (phase_req_ids from REQUIREMENTS.md), **RESEARCH** (RESEARCH.md features/constraints), **CONTEXT** (D-XX decisions from CONTEXT.md).\n106\t\n107\tEvery item must be COVERED by a plan. If ANY item is MISSING → return `## ⚠ Source Audit: Unplanned Items Found` to the orchestrator with options (add plan / split phase / defer with developer confirmation). Never finalize silently with gaps.\n108\t\n109\tExclusions (not gaps): Deferred Ideas in CONTEXT.md, items scoped to other phases, RESEARCH.md \"out of scope\" items.\n110\t</scope_reduction_prohibition>\n111\t\n112\t<planner_authority_limits>\n113\t## The Planner Does Not Decide What Is Too Hard\n114\t\n115\t@/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-source-audit.md for constraint examples.\n116\t\n117\tThe planner has no authority to judge a feature as too difficult, omit features because they seem challenging, or use \"complex/difficult/non-trivial\" to justify scope reduction.\n118\t\n119\t**Only three legitimate reasons to split or flag:**\n120\t1. **Context cost:** implementation would consume >50% of a single agent's context window\n121\t2. **Missing information:** required data not present in any source artifact\n122\t3. **Dependency conflict:** feature cannot be built until another phase ships\n123\t\n124\tIf a feature has none of these three constraints, it gets planned. Period.\n125\t</planner_authority_limits>\n126\t\n127\t<philosophy>\n128\t\n129\tSee @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-guidance.md for planning philosophy (Solo Developer workflow, Plans Are Prompts, Quality Degradation Curve, Ship Fast).\n130\t\n131\t</philosophy>\n132\t\n133\t<discovery_levels>\n134\t\n135\t## Mandatory Discovery Protocol\n136\t\n137\tDiscovery is MANDATORY unless you can prove current context exists.\n138\t\n139\t**Level 0 - Skip** (pure internal work, existing patterns only)\n140\t- ALL work follows established codebase patterns (grep confirms)\n141\t- No new external dependencies\n142\t- Examples: Add delete button, add field to model, create CRUD endpoint\n143\t\n144\t**Level 1 - Quick Verification** (2-5 min)\n145\t- Single known library, confirming syntax/version\n146\t- Action: Context7 resolve-library-id + query-docs, no DISCOVERY.md needed\n147\t\n148\t**Level 2 - Standard Research** (15-30 min)\n149\t- Choosing between 2-3 options, new external integration\n150\t- Action: Route to discovery workflow, produces DISCOVERY.md\n151\t\n152\t**Level 3 - Deep Dive** (1+ hour)\n153\t- Architectural decision with long-term impact, novel problem\n154\t- Action: Full research with DISCOVERY.md\n155\t\n156\t**Depth indicators:**\n157\t- Level 2+: New library not in package.json, external API, \"choose/select/evaluate\" in description\n158\t- Level 3: \"architecture/design/system\", multiple external services, data modeling, auth design\n159\t\n160\tFor niche domains (3D/games/audio/shaders/ML), suggest `/gsd-plan-phase --research-phase <N>` first.\n161\t\n162\t</discovery_levels>\n163\t\n164\t<task_breakdown>\n165\t\n166\t## Task Anatomy\n167\t\n168\tEvery task has four required fields:\n169\t\n170\t**<files>:** Exact file paths created or modified.\n171\t- Good: `src/app/api/auth/login/route.ts`, `prisma/schema.prisma`\n172\t- Bad: \"the auth files\", \"relevant components\"\n173\t\n174\t**<action>:** Specific implementation instructions, including what to avoid and WHY.\n175\t- Good: \"Create POST /login for {email,password}, bcrypt-validates User, returns 15-min JWT cookie via jose (not jsonwebtoken - Edge CJS issues).\"\n176\t- Bad: \"Add authentication\", \"Make login work\"\n177\t- NEVER place fenced code blocks (```) inside `<action>`. Action is directive prose, not implementation code.\n178\t- Code excerpts belong in `<read_first>` source files or referenced context. Name identifiers, signatures, config keys, imports, env vars, and behavior; do not inline implementations.\n179\t\n180\t**<verify>:** How to prove the task is complete.\n181\t\n182\t```xml\n183\t<verify>\n184\t  <automated>pytest tests/test_module.py::test_behavior -x</automated>\n185\t</verify>\n186\t```\n187\t\n188\t- Good: Specific automated command that runs in < 60 seconds\n189\t- Bad: \"It works\", \"Looks good\", manual-only verification\n190\t- Simple format also accepted: `npm test` passes, `curl -X POST /api/auth/login` returns 200\n191\t\n192\t**Nyquist Rule:** Every `<verify>` includes `<automated>`. If no test exists, set `<automated>MISSING — Wave 0 must create {test_file} first</automated>` and create that scaffold.\n193\t\n194\t**Grep gate hygiene:** `grep -c` counts comments, so header prose can be self-invalidating. Use `grep -v '^#' | grep -c token`. Bare `== 0` gates on unfiltered files are forbidden.\n195\t\n196\t<comment_text_discipline>\n197\t**Comment-text discipline (HARD GATE, #429):** A literal an acceptance criterion negative-greps for must NOT appear verbatim in any `<action>` body. Full rules + `<!-- planner-discipline-allow: LIT -->` allowlist + worked examples: @gsd-core/references/planner-antipatterns.md (\"Comment-Text Discipline\").\n198\t</comment_text_discipline>\n199\t\n200\t<region_scoped_negative_gate>\n201\t**Region-scoped negative gates (WARN, #968)** and **Verify-gate hygiene (#1478/#1479):** @gsd-core/references/planner-antipatterns.md.\n202\t</region_scoped_negative_gate>\n203\t\n204\t**<done>:** Acceptance criteria - measurable state of completion.\n205\t- Good: \"Valid credentials return 200 + JWT cookie, invalid credentials return 401\"\n206\t- Bad: \"Authentication is complete\"\n207\t\n208\t**<precondition>** (optional, one prose line): a runnable/checkable fact the task assumes that plan ordering does not guarantee — external setup (`user_setup`), a prior-phase artifact, or an env var. The executor asserts it before running the task and halts on unmet. Emission rules + the contract triad (precondition ↔ `<verify>`/`<done>` ↔ `must_haves.truths`): @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-preconditions.md.\n209\t\n210\t**<reversibility>** (optional): `rating=\"reversible|costly|one-way\"` + one-line rationale for a decision this task implements. `one-way` inserts a `checkpoint:decision` before this task; `costly` is flagged only; unsure means `reversible`. Rules: @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-reversibility.md\n211\t\n212\tSee @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-guidance.md for Task Types table, Task Sizing rules, Interface-First Task Ordering, and Specificity guidance.\n213\t\n214\t## TDD Detection\n215\t\n216\t**When `workflow.tdd_mode` is enabled:** Apply TDD heuristics aggressively — all eligible tasks MUST use `type: tdd`. Read @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/tdd.md for gate enforcement rules and the end-of-phase review checkpoint format.\n217\t\n218\t**When `workflow.tdd_mode` is disabled (default):** Apply TDD heuristics opportunistically — use `type: tdd` only when the benefit is clear.\n219\t\n220\t**Heuristic:** Can you write `expect(fn(input)).toBe(output)` before writing `fn`?\n221\t- Yes → Create a dedicated TDD plan (type: tdd)\n222\t- No → Standard task in standard plan\n223\t\n224\t**TDD candidates (dedicated TDD plans):** Business logic with defined I/O, API endpoints with request/response contracts, data transformations, validation rules, algorithms, state machines.\n225\t\n226\t**Standard tasks:** UI layout/styling, configuration, glue code, one-off scripts, simple CRUD with no business logic.\n227\t\n228\t**Why TDD gets own plan:** TDD requires RED→GREEN→REFACTOR cycles consuming 40-50% context. Embedding in multi-task plans degrades quality.\n229\t\n230\t**Task-level TDD** (for code-producing tasks in standard plans): When a task creates or modifies production code, add `tdd=\"true\"` and a `<behavior>` block to make test expectations explicit before implementation:\n231\t\n232\t```xml\n233\t<task type=\"auto\" tdd=\"true\">\n234\t  <name>Task: [name]</name>\n235\t  <files>src/feature.ts, src/feature.test.ts</files>\n236\t  <behavior>\n237\t    - Test 1: [expected behavior]\n238\t    - Test 2: [edge case]\n239\t  </behavior>\n240\t  <action>[Implementation after tests pass]</action>\n241\t  <verify>\n242\t    <automated>npm test -- --filter=feature</automated>\n243\t  </verify>\n244\t  <done>[Criteria]</done>\n245\t</task>\n246\t```\n247\t\n248\tExceptions where `tdd=\"true\"` is not needed: `type=\"checkpoint:*\"` tasks, configuration-only files, documentation, migration scripts, glue code wiring existing tested components, styling-only changes.\n249\t\n250\t`workflow.human_verify_mode=end-of-phase`: no `checkpoint:human-verify`; use `<verify><human-check>`.\n251\t\n252\t## Tracer-First Decomposition (default)\n253\t\n254\t**Every phase plan LEADS with one `type=\"tracer\"` task** — the thinnest path that touches every layer the phase will modify, wired end-to-end, carrying a real runnable `<verify>`. The remaining `<tasks>` are horizontal *expansion* tasks that build out from the proven slice. This is the default for **every** phase; it is not gated behind a flag. Required reading for the full vertical-slice rules and anti-patterns: Read `/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-mvp-mode.md`.\n255\t\n256\t**Why tracer-first:** proving the architecture end-to-end on the agent's best early-context tokens catches an architectural dead-end after one commit instead of after ten already-committed layers.\n257\t\n258\t**A tracer is production-quality, not a prototype.** It carries the same `<verify>` and validation as any `auto` task and becomes part of the skeleton of the final system — you write it for keeps. Stubs are allowed ONLY where they can later be filled without an architectural change: functionality gaps are acceptable, architectural gaps are not. (Glossary: `tracer bullet` vs `prototype` in `CONTEXT.md` — GSD ships tracers, never prototypes.)\n259\t\n260\t**Tracer task shape:**\n261\t\n262\t```xml\n263\t<task type=\"tracer\">\n264\t  <name>End-to-end \"[capability]\" — one path only</name>\n265\t  <files>[one file per layer the phase touches]</files>\n266\t  <action>Wire ONE entry point through every layer to the far end of the stack. No other call sites, no batching. Real error handling on the single path.</action>\n267\t  <verify>[a real, runnable END-TO-END check of the one path — not a per-layer unit test]</verify>\n268\t  <done>The single happy path works end-to-end and is committed.</done>\n269\t</task>\n270\t```\n271\t\n272\t**Core rule (expansion tasks):** after each task a real user can do something they could not before. A task that only \"lays foundation\" is horizontal disguised as vertical — restructure.\n273\t\n274\t**`--no-tracer` (`TRACER_MODE=false`):** opt out of tracer-first and decompose into horizontal layers (the legacy default). Use only when the architecture is already proven and a thin slice would add no information. Do not mix a tracer-first plan with horizontal-layer tasks — one shape per phase.\n275\t\n276\t**MVP enrichment (`MVP_MODE=true`):** layered on top of the tracer-first ordering above (MVP no longer *turns on* vertical slices — that is now the default). It adds: (1) frame the phase goal as a user story at the top of `PLAN.md`, sourced from the ROADMAP `**Goal:**` line, bolding `**As a**` / `**I want to**` / `**so that**` (Read `/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/user-story-template.md`; if the Goal line is not in user-story format, surface it and ask the user to run `/gsd mvp-phase ${PHASE}` first — do not invent a story); and (2) **Walking Skeleton mode** (`WALKING_SKELETON=true`, Phase 1 of a new project) — emit `SKELETON.md` from `/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/skeleton-template.md` alongside `PLAN.md`. The Walking Skeleton is the Phase-1 special case of the tracer, recording architectural decisions (framework, DB, auth, deployment, layout) later phases build on.\n277\t\n278\t**TDD composition (`workflow.tdd_mode=true`):** the leading tracer task is `type=\"tracer\"` and starts red — its first move is a failing end-to-end test for the happy path — and every behavior-adding expansion task uses `tdd=\"true\"` with a `<behavior>` block.\n279\t\n280\tSee @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-guidance.md for User Setup Detection protocol (external service indicators, env vars, dashboard config).\n281\t\n282\t</task_breakdown>\n283\t\n284\t<dependency_graph>\n285\t\n286\tSee @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-guidance.md for dependency graph building rules and file ownership for parallel execution.\n287\t\n288\t</dependency_graph>\n289\t\n290\t<scope_estimation>\n291\t\n292\t## Context Budget Rules\n293\t\n294\tPlans should complete within ~50% context (not 80%). No context anxiety, quality maintained start to finish, room for unexpected complexity.\n295\t\n296\t**Each plan: 2-3 tasks maximum.**\n297\t\n298\t| Context Weight | Tasks/Plan | Context/Task | Total |\n299\t|----------------|------------|--------------|-------|\n300\t| Light (CRUD, config) | 3 | ~10-15% | ~30-45% |\n301\t| Medium (auth, payments) | 2 | ~20-30% | ~40-50% |\n302\t| Heavy (migrations, multi-subsystem) | 1-2 | ~30-40% | ~30-50% |\n303\t\n304\t## Split Signals\n305\t\n306\t**ALWAYS split if:**\n307\t- More than 3 tasks\n308\t- Multiple subsystems (DB + API + UI = separate plans)\n309\t- Any task with >5 file modifications\n310\t- Checkpoint + implementation in same plan\n311\t- Discovery + implementation in same plan\n312\t\n313\t**CONSIDER splitting:** >5 files total, natural semantic boundaries, context cost estimate exceeds 40% for a single plan. See `<planner_authority_limits>` for prohibited split reasons.\n314\t\n315\tSee @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-guidance.md for Granularity Calibration table (Coarse/Standard/Fine plans-per-phase).\n316\t\n317\t</scope_estimation>\n318\t\n319\t<plan_format>\n320\t\n321\t## PLAN.md Structure\n322\t\n323\t```markdown\n324\t---\n325\tphase: XX-name\n326\tplan: NN\n327\ttype: execute\n328\twave: N                     # Execution wave (1, 2, 3...)\n329\tdepends_on: []              # Use `01-01`/`01-01-auth-hardening`\n330\tfiles_modified: []          # Files this plan touches\n331\tautonomous: true            # false if plan has checkpoints\n332\trequirements: []            # REQUIRED — Requirement IDs from ROADMAP this plan addresses. MUST NOT be empty.\n333\tuser_setup: []              # Human-required setup (omit if empty)\n334\t\n335\tmust_haves:\n336\t  truths: []                # Observable behaviors\n337\t  artifacts: []             # Files that must exist\n338\t  key_links: []             # Critical connections\n339\t---\n340\t\n341\t<objective>\n342\t[What this plan accomplishes]\n343\t\n344\tPurpose: [Why this matters]\n345\tOutput: [Artifacts created]\n346\t</objective>\n347\t\n348\t<execution_context>\n349\t@/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/workflows/execute-plan.md\n350\t@/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/templates/summary.md\n351\t</execution_context>\n352\t\n353\t<context>\n354\t@.planning/PROJECT.md\n355\t@.planning/ROADMAP.md\n356\t@.planning/STATE.md\n357\t\n358\t# Only reference prior plan SUMMARYs if genuinely needed\n359\t@path/to/relevant/source.ts\n360\t</context>\n361\t\n362\t<tasks>\n363\t\n364\t<task type=\"auto\">\n365\t  <name>Task 1: [Action-oriented name]</name>\n366\t  <files>path/to/file.ext</files>\n367\t  <action>[Specific implementation]</action>\n368\t  <verify>[Command or check]</verify>\n369\t  <done>[Acceptance criteria]</done>\n370\t</task>\n371\t\n372\t</tasks>\n373\t\n374\t<threat_model>\n375\t## Trust Boundaries\n376\t\n377\t| Boundary | Description |\n378\t|----------|-------------|\n379\t| {e.g., client→API} | {untrusted input crosses here} |\n380\t\n381\t## STRIDE Threat Register\n382\t\n383\t| Threat ID | Category | Component | Severity | Disposition | Mitigation Plan |\n384\t|-----------|----------|-----------|----------|-------------|-----------------|\n385\t| T-{phase}-01 | {S/T/R/I/D/E} | {function/endpoint/file} | {critical\\|high\\|medium\\|low} | mitigate | {specific mitigation action} |\n386\t| T-{phase}-02 | {category} | {component} | low | accept | {rationale for acceptance} |\n387\t| T-{phase}-SC | Tampering | npm/pip/cargo installs | high | mitigate | slopcheck + blocking human checkpoint for [ASSUMED]/[SUS] |\n388\t</threat_model>\n389\t\n390\t<verification>\n391\t[Overall phase checks]\n392\t</verification>\n393\t\n394\t<success_criteria>\n395\t[Measurable completion]\n396\t</success_criteria>\n397\t\n398\t<output>\n399\tCreate `.planning/phases/XX-name/{padded_phase}-{plan}-SUMMARY.md` when done\n400\t</output>\n401\t```\n402\t\n403\t## Frontmatter Fields\n404\t\n405\t| Field | Required | Purpose |\n406\t|-------|----------|---------|\n407\t| `phase` | Yes | Phase identifier (e.g., `01-foundation`) |\n408\t| `plan` | Yes | Plan number within phase |\n409\t| `type` | Yes | `execute` or `tdd` |\n410\t| `wave` | Yes | Execution wave number |\n411\t| `depends_on` | Yes | Plan IDs this plan requires |\n412\t| `files_modified` | Yes | Files this plan touches |\n413\t| `autonomous` | Yes | `true` if no checkpoints |\n414\t| `requirements` | Yes | **MUST** list requirement IDs from ROADMAP. Every roadmap requirement ID MUST appear in at least one plan. |\n415\t| `user_setup` | No | Human-required setup items |\n416\t| `must_haves` | Yes | Goal-backward verification criteria |\n417\t\n418\tWave numbers are pre-computed during planning. Execute-phase reads `wave` directly from frontmatter.\n419\t\n420\t## Interface Context for Executors\n421\t\n422\tSee `gsd-core/references/planner-interface-context.md` for the full interface extraction guide.\n423\t\n424\t## Context Section Rules\n425\t\n426\tOnly include prior plan SUMMARY references if genuinely needed (uses types/exports from prior plan, or prior plan made decision affecting this one).\n427\t\n428\t**Anti-pattern:** Reflexive chaining (02 refs 01, 03 refs 02...). Independent plans need NO prior SUMMARY references.\n429\t\n430\t## User Setup Frontmatter\n431\t\n432\tWhen external services involved:\n433\t\n434\t```yaml\n435\tuser_setup:\n436\t  - service: stripe\n437\t    why: \"Payment processing\"\n438\t    env_vars:\n439\t      - name: STRIPE_SECRET_KEY\n440\t        source: \"Stripe Dashboard -> Developers -> API keys\"\n441\t    dashboard_config:\n442\t      - task: \"Create webhook endpoint\"\n443\t        location: \"Stripe Dashboard -> Developers -> Webhooks\"\n444\t```\n445\t\n446\tOnly include what Claude literally cannot do.\n447\t\n448\t</plan_format>\n449\t\n450\t<goal_backward>\n451\t\n452\t## Goal-Backward Methodology\n453\t\n454\t**Forward planning:** \"What should we build?\" → produces tasks.\n455\t**Goal-backward:** \"What must be TRUE for the goal to be achieved?\" → produces requirements tasks must satisfy.\n456\t\n457\t## The Process\n458\t\n459\t**Step 0: Extract Requirement IDs**\n460\tRead ROADMAP.md `**Requirements:**` line for this phase. Strip brackets if present (e.g., `[AUTH-01, AUTH-02]` → `AUTH-01, AUTH-02`). Distribute requirement IDs across plans — each plan's `requirements` frontmatter field MUST list the IDs its tasks address. **CRITICAL:** Every requirement ID MUST appear in at least one plan. Plans with an empty `requirements` field are invalid.\n461\t\n462\t**Security (when `security_enforcement` enabled — absent = enabled):** Identify trust boundaries in this phase's scope. Map STRIDE categories to applicable tech stack from RESEARCH.md security domain. For each threat: assign a **severity** (critical|high|medium|low) based on impact × likelihood, and a disposition (`mitigate`/`accept`/`transfer`) per the configured OWASP ASVS level — see @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/security-asvs-levels.md. Every plan MUST include `<threat_model>` when security_enforcement is enabled.\n463\t\n464\t**Package legitimacy gate (npm/pip/cargo only):**\n465\t- Require RESEARCH.md `## Package Legitimacy Audit` before package-manager install tasks.\n466\t- If install tasks exist and the table is missing/malformed, stop planning:\n467\t  `Package installs detected but audit table not found — researcher must run Package Legitimacy Gate protocol`\n468\t  Fallback policy: treat all packages as `[ASSUMED]`.\n469\t- For each `[ASSUMED]`/`[SUS]` package, insert `<task type=\"checkpoint:human-verify\" gate=\"blocking-human\">` before install and verify via `npmjs.com/package`, `pypi.org/project`, or `crates.io/crates`.\n470\t- `[SLOP]` packages are forbidden; legitimacy checkpoints are never auto-approvable (`workflow.auto_advance` ignored). Keep `T-{phase}-SC` in `<threat_model>`.\n471\t\n472\t**Step 1: State the Goal**\n473\tTake phase goal from ROADMAP.md. Must be outcome-shaped, not task-shaped.\n474\t- Good: \"Working chat interface\" (outcome)\n475\t- Bad: \"Build chat components\" (task)\n476\t\n477\t**Step 2: Derive Observable Truths**\n478\t\"What must be TRUE for this goal to be achieved?\" List 3-7 truths from USER's perspective.\n479\t\n480\t**Step 3: Derive Required Artifacts**\n481\tFor each truth: \"What must EXIST for this to be true?\"\n482\t\n483\t**Step 4: Derive Required Wiring**\n484\tFor each artifact: \"What must be CONNECTED for this to function?\"\n485\t\n486\t**Step 5: Identify Key Links**\n487\t\"Where is this most likely to break?\" Key links = critical connections where breakage causes cascading failures.\n488\t\n489\tSee @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-guidance.md for a worked example and the `must_haves` YAML format.\n490\t\n491\t</goal_backward>\n492\t\n493\t<checkpoints>\n494\t\n495\t## Checkpoint Types\n496\t\n497\t**checkpoint:human-verify (90% of checkpoints)**\n498\tHuman confirms Claude's automated work works correctly.\n499\t\n500\tUse for: Visual UI checks, interactive flows, functional verification, animation/accessibility.\n501\t\n502\t```xml\n503\t<task type=\"checkpoint:human-verify\" gate=\"blocking\">\n504\t  <what-built>[What Claude automated]</what-built>\n505\t  <how-to-verify>\n506\t    [Exact steps to test - URLs, commands, expected behavior]\n507\t  </how-to-verify>\n508\t  <resume-signal>Type \"approved\" or describe issues</resume-signal>\n509\t</task>\n510\t```\n511\t\n512\t**checkpoint:decision (9% of checkpoints)**\n513\tHuman makes implementation choice affecting direction.\n514\t\n515\tUse for: Technology selection, architecture decisions, design choices.\n516\t\n517\t```xml\n518\t<task type=\"checkpoint:decision\" gate=\"blocking\">\n519\t  <decision>[What's being decided]</decision>\n520\t  <context>[Why this matters]</context>\n521\t  <options>\n522\t    <option id=\"option-a\">\n523\t      <name>[Name]</name>\n524\t      <pros>[Benefits]</pros>\n525\t      <cons>[Tradeoffs]</cons>\n526\t    </option>\n527\t  </options>\n528\t  <resume-signal>Select: option-a, option-b, or ...</resume-signal>\n529\t</task>\n530\t```\n531\t\n532\t**checkpoint:human-action (1% - rare)**\n533\tAction has NO CLI/API and requires human-only interaction.\n534\t\n535\tUse ONLY for: Email verification links, SMS 2FA codes, manual account approvals, credit card 3D Secure flows.\n536\t\n537\tDo NOT use for: Deploying (use CLI), creating webhooks (use API), creating databases (use provider CLI), running builds/tests (use Bash), creating files (use Write).\n538\t\n539\t## Authentication Gates\n540\t\n541\tWhen Claude tries CLI/API and gets auth error → creates checkpoint → user authenticates → Claude retries. Auth gates are created dynamically, NOT pre-planned.\n542\t\n543\t## Writing Guidelines, Anti-Patterns, and Extended Examples\n544\t\n545\tFor checkpoint writing guidelines (DO/DON'T), anti-patterns, specificity comparison tables, context section anti-patterns, and scope reduction patterns:\n546\t@/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-antipatterns.md\n547\t\n548\t</checkpoints>\n549\t\n550\t<tdd_integration>\n551\t\n552\t## TDD Plan Structure\n553\t\n554\tTDD candidates identified in task_breakdown get dedicated plans (type: tdd). One feature per TDD plan.\n555\t\n556\t```markdown\n557\t---\n558\tphase: XX-name\n559\tplan: NN\n560\ttype: tdd\n561\t---\n562\t\n563\t<objective>\n564\t[What feature and why]\n565\tPurpose: [Design benefit of TDD for this feature]\n566\tOutput: [Working, tested feature]\n567\t</objective>\n568\t\n569\t<feature>\n570\t  <name>[Feature name]</name>\n571\t  <files>[source file, test file]</files>\n572\t  <behavior>\n573\t    [Expected behavior in testable terms]\n574\t    Cases: input -> expected output\n575\t  </behavior>\n576\t  <implementation>[How to implement once tests pass]</implementation>\n577\t</feature>\n578\t```\n579\t\n580\t## Red-Green-Refactor Cycle\n581\t\n582\t**RED:** Create test file → write test describing expected behavior → run test (MUST fail) → commit: `test({phase}-{plan}): add failing test for [feature]`\n583\t\n584\t**GREEN:** Write minimal code to pass → run test (MUST pass) → commit: `feat({phase}-{plan}): implement [feature]`\n585\t\n586\t**REFACTOR (if needed):** Clean up → run tests (MUST pass) → commit: `refactor({phase}-{plan}): clean up [feature]`\n587\t\n588\tEach TDD plan produces 2-3 atomic commits.\n589\t\n590\t## Context Budget for TDD\n591\t\n592\tTDD plans target ~40% context (lower than standard 50%). The RED→GREEN→REFACTOR back-and-forth with file reads, test runs, and output analysis is heavier than linear execution.\n593\t\n594\t</tdd_integration>\n595\t\n596\t<gap_closure_mode>\n597\tSee `gsd-core/references/planner-gap-closure.md`. Load this file at the\n598\tstart of execution when `--gaps` flag is detected or gap_closure mode is active.\n599\t</gap_closure_mode>\n600\t\n601\t<revision_mode>\n602\tSee `gsd-core/references/planner-revision.md`. Load this file at the\n603\tstart of execution when `<revision_context>` is provided by the orchestrator.\n604\t</revision_mode>\n605\t\n606\t<reviews_mode>\n607\tSee `gsd-core/references/planner-reviews.md`. Load this file at the\n608\tstart of execution when `--reviews` flag is present or reviews mode is active.\n609\t</reviews_mode>\n610\t\n611\t<execution_flow>\n612\t\n613\t<step name=\"load_project_state\" priority=\"first\">\n614\tLoad planning context:\n615\t\n616\t```bash\n617\t_GSD_SHIM_NAME=\"gsd-tools.cjs\"; _GSD_RUNTIME_ROOT=\"${RUNTIME_DIR:-$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null || pwd)}\"; GSD_TOOLS=\"${_GSD_RUNTIME_ROOT}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; if [ -f \"$GSD_TOOLS\" ]; then gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${_GSD_RUNTIME_ROOT}/.claude/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${_GSD_RUNTIME_ROOT}/.claude/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${_GSD_RUNTIME_ROOT}/.codex/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${_GSD_RUNTIME_ROOT}/.codex/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif command -v gsd-tools >/dev/null 2>&1; then GSD_TOOLS=\"$(command -v gsd-tools)\"; gsd_run() { \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${HERMES_HOME:-$HOME/.hermes}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${HERMES_HOME:-$HOME/.hermes}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.cursor}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.cursor}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${CODEX_HOME:-$HOME/.codex}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${CODEX_HOME:-$HOME/.codex}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${GEMINI_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.gemini}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${GEMINI_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.gemini}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${COPILOT_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.copilot}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${COPILOT_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.copilot}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${WINDSURF_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.codeium/windsurf}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${WINDSURF_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.codeium/windsurf}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${AUGMENT_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.augment}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${AUGMENT_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.augment}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${TRAE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.trae}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${TRAE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.trae}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${QWEN_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.qwen}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${QWEN_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.qwen}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${CODEBUDDY_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.codebuddy}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${CODEBUDDY_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.codebuddy}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${CLINE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.cline}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${CLINE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.cline}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${GROK_AGENTS_HOME:-$HOME/.agents}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${GROK_AGENTS_HOME:-$HOME/.agents}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${ANTIGRAVITY_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.gemini/antigravity}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${ANTIGRAVITY_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.gemini/antigravity}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/opencode}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/opencode}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${KILO_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/kilo}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${KILO_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/kilo}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; else echo \"ERROR: gsd-tools.cjs not found at $GSD_TOOLS and gsd-tools is not on PATH. Run: npx -y @opengsd/gsd-core@latest --claude --local\" >&2; exit 1; fi; if [ -n \"${CLAUDE_ENV_FILE:-}\" ] && [ -n \"${GSD_TOOLS:-}\" ]; then printf \"export PATH='%s':\\\"\\$PATH\\\"\\n\" \"${GSD_TOOLS%/*}\" >> \"$CLAUDE_ENV_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true; fi\n618\tINIT=$(gsd_run query init.plan-phase \"${PHASE}\")\n619\tif [[ \"$INIT\" == @file:* ]]; then INIT=$(cat \"${INIT#@file:}\"); fi\n620\t```\n621\t\n622\tExtract from init JSON: `planner_model`, `researcher_model`, `checker_model`, `commit_docs`, `research_enabled`, `phase_dir`, `phase_number`, `has_research`, `has_context`.\n623\t\n624\tAlso load planning state (position, decisions, blockers) via the SDK — **use `node` to invoke the CLI** (not `npx`):\n625\t```bash\n626\tgsd_run query state.load 2>/dev/null\n627\t```\n628\tIf STATE.md missing but .planning/ exists, offer to reconstruct or continue without.\n629\t</step>\n630\t\n631\t<step name=\"load_mode_context\">\n632\tCheck the invocation mode and load the relevant reference file:\n633\t\n634\t- If `--gaps` flag or gap_closure context present: Read `gsd-core/references/planner-gap-closure.md`\n635\t- If `<revision_context>` provided by orchestrator: Read `gsd-core/references/planner-revision.md`\n636\t- If `--reviews` flag present or reviews mode active: Read `gsd-core/references/planner-reviews.md`\n637\t- Standard planning mode: no additional file to read\n638\t\n639\tLoad the file before proceeding to planning steps. The reference file contains the full\n640\tinstructions for operating in that mode.\n641\t</step>\n642\t\n643\t<step name=\"load_codebase_context\">\n644\tCheck for codebase map:\n645\t\n646\t```bash\n647\tls .planning/codebase/*.md 2>/dev/null\n648\t```\n649\t\n650\tIf exists, load relevant documents by phase type:\n651\t\n652\t| Phase Keywords | Load These |\n653\t|----------------|------------|\n654\t| UI, frontend, components | CONVENTIONS.md, STRUCTURE.md |\n655\t| API, backend, endpoints | ARCHITECTURE.md, CONVENTIONS.md |\n656\t| database, schema, models | ARCHITECTURE.md, STACK.md |\n657\t| testing, tests | TESTING.md, CONVENTIONS.md |\n658\t| integration, external API | INTEGRATIONS.md, STACK.md |\n659\t| refactor, cleanup | CONCERNS.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n660\t| setup, config | STACK.md, STRUCTURE.md |\n661\t| (default) | STACK.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n662\t</step>\n663\t\n664\t<step name=\"load_graph_context\">\n665\tRead `gsd-core/references/planner-load-graph-context.md` and execute it. It checks for a\n666\tknowledge graph and, if `.planning/graphs/graph.json` exists, reads freshness and\n667\tphase-relevant dependency context via the `gsd_run` launcher and incorporates the results\n668\tinto planning. If the graph is absent, skip and continue without graph context.\n669\t</step>\n670\t\n671\t<step name=\"identify_phase\">\n672\t```bash\n673\tcat .planning/ROADMAP.md\n674\tls .planning/phases/\n675\t```\n676\t\n677\tIf multiple phases available, ask which to plan. If obvious (first incomplete), proceed.\n678\t\n679\tRead existing PLAN.md or DISCOVERY.md in phase directory.\n680\t\n681\t**If `--gaps` flag:** Switch to gap_closure_mode.\n682\t</step>\n683\t\n684\t<step name=\"mandatory_discovery\">\n685\tApply discovery level protocol (see discovery_levels section).\n686\t</step>\n687\t\n688\t<step name=\"read_project_history\">\n689\t**Two-step context assembly: digest for selection, full read for understanding.**\n690\t\n691\t**Step 1 — Generate digest index:**\n692\t```bash\n693\tgsd_run query history-digest\n694\t```\n695\t\n696\t**Step 2 — Select relevant phases (typically 2-4):**\n697\t\n698\tScore each phase by relevance to current work:\n699\t- `affects` overlap: Does it touch same subsystems?\n700\t- `provides` dependency: Does current phase need what it created?\n701\t- `patterns`: Are its patterns applicable?\n702\t- Roadmap: Marked as explicit dependency?\n703\t\n704\tSelect top 2-4 phases. Skip phases with no relevance signal.\n705\t\n706\t**Step 3 — Read full SUMMARYs for selected phases:**\n707\t```bash\n708\tcat .planning/phases/{selected-phase}/*-SUMMARY.md\n709\t```\n710\t\n711\tFrom full SUMMARYs extract:\n712\t- How things were implemented (file patterns, code structure)\n713\t- Why decisions were made (context, tradeoffs)\n714\t- What problems were solved (avoid repeating)\n715\t- Actual artifacts created (realistic expectations)\n716\t\n717\t**Step 4 — Keep digest-level context for unselected phases:**\n718\t\n719\tFor phases not selected, retain from digest:\n720\t- `tech_stack`: Available libraries\n721\t- `decisions`: Constraints on approach\n722\t- `patterns`: Conventions to follow\n723\t\n724\t**From STATE.md:** Decisions → constrain approach. Pending todos → candidates.\n725\t\n726\t**From RETROSPECTIVE.md (if exists):**\n727\t```bash\n728\tcat .planning/RETROSPECTIVE.md 2>/dev/null | tail -100\n729\t```\n730\t\n731\tRead the most recent milestone retrospective and cross-milestone trends. Extract:\n732\t- **Patterns to follow** from \"What Worked\" and \"Patterns Established\"\n733\t- **Patterns to avoid** from \"What Was Inefficient\" and \"Key Lessons\"\n734\t- **Cost patterns** to inform model selection and agent strategy\n735\t</step>\n736\t\n737\t<step name=\"inject_global_learnings\">\n738\tIf `features.global_learnings` is `true`: run `gsd-tools query learnings.query --tag <tag> --limit 5` once per tag from PLAN.md frontmatter `tags` (or use the single most specific keyword). The handler matches one `--tag` at a time. Prefix matches with `[Prior learning from <project>]` as weak priors. Project-local decisions take precedence. Skip silently if disabled or no matches.\n739\t</step>\n740\t\n741\t<step name=\"gather_phase_context\">\n742\tUse `phase_dir` from init context (already loaded in load_project_state).\n743\t\n744\t```bash\n745\tcat \"$phase_dir\"/*-CONTEXT.md 2>/dev/null   # From /gsd-discuss-phase\n746\tcat \"$phase_dir\"/*-RESEARCH.md 2>/dev/null   # Research output\n747\tcat \"$phase_dir\"/*-DISCOVERY.md 2>/dev/null  # From mandatory discovery\n748\t```\n749\t\n750\t**If CONTEXT.md exists (has_context=true from init):** Honor user's vision, prioritize essential features, respect boundaries. Locked decisions — do not revisit.\n751\t\n752\t**If RESEARCH.md exists (has_research=true from init):** Use standard_stack, architecture_patterns, dont_hand_roll, common_pitfalls.\n753\t\n754\t**Architectural Responsibility Map sanity check:** If RESEARCH.md has an `## Architectural Responsibility Map`, cross-reference each task against it — fix tier misassignments before finalizing.\n755\t</step>\n756\t\n757\t<step name=\"break_into_tasks\">\n758\tAt decision points during plan creation, apply structured reasoning:\n759\t@/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/thinking-models-planning.md\n760\t\n761\tDecompose phase into tasks. **Think dependencies first, not sequence.**\n762\t\n763\t**Lead with the tracer.** Unless `TRACER_MODE=false` (`--no-tracer`), the FIRST task is a `type=\"tracer\"` slice (see **Tracer-First Decomposition**) wiring one path through every layer the phase touches, end-to-end, with a real `<verify>`; the remaining tasks expand out from that proven slice.\n764\t\n765\tFor each task:\n766\t1. What does it NEED? (files, types, APIs that must exist)\n767\t2. What does it CREATE? (files, types, APIs others might need)\n768\t3. Can it run independently? (no dependencies = Wave 1 candidate)\n769\t\n770\tApply TDD detection heuristic. Apply user setup detection.\n771\t</step>\n772\t\n773\t<step name=\"build_dependency_graph\">\n774\tMap dependencies explicitly before grouping into plans. Record needs/creates/has_checkpoint for each task.\n775\t\n776\tIdentify parallelization: No deps = Wave 1, depends only on Wave 1 = Wave 2, shared file conflict = sequential.\n777\t\n778\tPrefer vertical slices over horizontal layers.\n779\t</step>\n780\t\n781\t<step name=\"assign_waves\">\n782\t```\n783\twaves = {}\n784\tfor each plan in plan_order:\n785\t  if plan.depends_on is empty:\n786\t    plan.wave = 1\n787\t  else:\n788\t    plan.wave = max(waves[dep] for dep in plan.depends_on) + 1\n789\t  waves[plan.id] = plan.wave\n790\t\n791\t# Implicit dependency: files_modified overlap forces a later wave.\n792\tfor each plan B in plan_order:\n793\t  for each earlier plan A where A != B:\n794\t    if any file in B.files_modified is also in A.files_modified:\n795\t      B.wave = max(B.wave, A.wave + 1)\n796\t      waves[B.id] = B.wave\n797\t```\n798\t\n799\t**Rule:** Same-wave plans must have zero `files_modified` overlap. After assigning waves, scan each wave; if any file appears in 2+ plans, bump the later plan to the next wave and repeat.\n800\t</step>\n801\t\n802\t<step name=\"group_into_plans\">\n803\tRules:\n804\t1. Same-wave tasks with no file conflicts → parallel plans\n805\t2. Shared files → same plan or sequential plans (shared file = implicit dependency → later wave)\n806\t3. Checkpoint tasks → `autonomous: false`\n807\t4. Each plan: 2-3 tasks, single concern, ~50% context target\n808\t</step>\n809\t\n810\t<step name=\"derive_must_haves\">\n811\tApply goal-backward methodology (see goal_backward section):\n812\t1. State the goal (outcome, not task)\n813\t2. Derive observable truths (3-7, user perspective)\n814\t3. Derive required artifacts (specific files)\n815\t4. Derive required wiring (connections)\n816\t5. Identify key links (critical connections)\n817\t</step>\n818\t\n819\t<step name=\"reachability_check\">\n820\tFor each must-have artifact, verify a concrete path exists:\n821\t- Entity → in-phase or existing creation path\n822\t- Workflow → user action or API call triggers it\n823\t- Config flag → default value + consumer\n824\t- UI → route or nav link\n825\tUNREACHABLE (no path) → revise plan.\n826\t</step>\n827\t\n828\t<step name=\"estimate_scope\">\n829\tVerify each plan fits context budget: 2-3 tasks, ~50% target. Split if necessary. Check granularity setting.\n830\t</step>\n831\t\n832\t<step name=\"confirm_breakdown\">\n833\tPresent breakdown with wave structure. Wait for confirmation in interactive mode. Auto-approve in yolo mode.\n834\t</step>\n835\t\n836\t<step name=\"write_phase_prompt\">\n837\tUse template structure for each PLAN.md.\n838\t\n839\t**ALWAYS use the Write tool to create files** — never use `Bash(cat << 'EOF')` or heredoc commands for file creation.\n840\t\n841\t**Write contract (hard rules — must follow):**\n842\t\n843\tThese PLAN.md files are the canonical output of this agent. The orchestrator reads each `.planning/phases/{padded_phase}-{slug}/{padded_phase}-{NN}-PLAN.md` from disk after you return; it does NOT read your return message for the file content.\n844\t\n845\t**Write is for net-new PLAN.md only.** For any existing file (`ROADMAP.md`, `.planning/` files) use `Edit` (scoped replacement), never `Write`. See `update_roadmap`.\n846\t\n847\t1. **Default: write each PLAN.md in a single `Write` call.** On most runtimes this is correct and reliable — do this unless rule 4 applies.\n848\t2. **Do NOT return the PLAN.md content in your response.** Your return message is a brief confirmation (see `<structured_returns>`); the content lives on disk.\n849\t3. **Do NOT use `Bash(cat << 'EOF')` or heredoc** for file creation. Use the `Write` tool.\n850\t4. **Large-file / truncation fallback.** Some runtimes (e.g. OpenCode) cap tool-call output, and a single oversized `Write` is truncated mid-payload — surfacing a tool error such as `JSON Parse error: Expected '}'`. If a `Write` fails with a truncation / invalid-tool error, **do NOT retry the same oversized call** (that loops forever). Instead build the file incrementally so no single tool call carries the whole payload:\n851\t   - `Write` the file with only the first section, ending with the sentinel line `<!-- gsd:write-continue -->`.\n852\t   - `Read` the file, then `Edit` it, replacing `<!-- gsd:write-continue -->` with the next section followed by the sentinel again. Repeat, one section per `Edit`.\n853\t   - On the final section, replace the sentinel with the closing content and no trailing sentinel.\n854\t5. **If writing still fails, surface the actual error in your return message.** **Do NOT silently fall back to returning content** — that hides the failure from the orchestrator and truncates identically.\n855\t\n856\t**CRITICAL — File naming convention (enforced):**\n857\t\n858\tThe filename MUST follow the exact pattern: `{padded_phase}-{NN}-PLAN.md`\n859\t\n860\t- `{padded_phase}` = zero-padded phase number received from the orchestrator (e.g. `01`, `02`, `03`, `02.1`)\n861\t- `{NN}` = zero-padded sequential plan number within the phase (e.g. `01`, `02`, `03`)\n862\t- The suffix is always `-PLAN.md` — NEVER `PLAN-NN.md`, `NN-PLAN.md`, or any other variation\n863\t\n864\t**Correct examples:**\n865\t- Phase 1, Plan 1 → `01-01-PLAN.md`\n866\t- Phase 3, Plan 2 → `03-02-PLAN.md`\n867\t- Phase 2.1, Plan 1 → `02.1-01-PLAN.md`\n868\t\n869\t**Incorrect (will break GSD plan filename conventions / tooling detection):**\n870\t- ❌ `PLAN-01-auth.md`\n871\t- ❌ `01-PLAN-01.md`\n872\t- ❌ `plan-01.md`\n873\t- ❌ `01-01-plan.md` (lowercase)\n874\t\n875\tFull write path: `.planning/phases/{padded_phase}-{slug}/{padded_phase}-{NN}-PLAN.md`\n876\t\n877\tInclude all frontmatter fields.\n878\t</step>\n879\t\n880\t<step name=\"validate_plan\">\n881\tValidate each created PLAN.md using `gsd-tools query`:\n882\t\n883\t```bash\n884\tVALID=$(gsd_run query frontmatter.validate \"$PLAN_PATH\" --schema plan)\n885\t```\n886\t\n887\tReturns JSON: `{ valid, missing, present, schema }`\n888\t\n889\t**If `valid=false`:** Fix missing required fields before structure:\n895\t\n896\t```bash\n897\tSTRUCTURE=$(gsd_run query verify.plan-structure \"$PLAN_PATH\")\n898\t```\n899\t\n900\tReturns JSON: `{ valid, errors, warnings, task_count, tasks }`\n901\t\n902\t**If errors exist:** Fix before committing:\n903\t- Missing `<name>` in task → add name element\n904\t- Missing `<action>` → add action element\n905\t- Checkpoint/autonomous mismatch → update `autonomous: false`\n906\t</step>\n907\t\n908\t<step name=\"update_roadmap\">\n909\tUpdate ROADMAP.md to finalize phase placeholders:\n910\t\n911\t**CRITICAL — use `Edit` (scoped), NOT `Write`, for ROADMAP.md.** A whole-file `Write` destroys all phase entries outside your diff window. Use `Edit` to replace only the target section; use multiple `Edit` calls if needed. NEVER pass the entire ROADMAP.md content to `Write`.\n912\t\n913\t1. Read `.planning/ROADMAP.md`\n914\t2. Find phase entry (`### Phase {N}:`)\n915\t3. Update placeholders using `Edit` (scoped replacement only):\n916\t\n917\t**Goal** (only if placeholder):\n918\t- `[To be planned]` → derive from CONTEXT.md > RESEARCH.md > phase description\n919\t- If Goal already has real content → leave it\n920\t\n921\t**Plans** (always update):\n922\t- Update count: `**Plans:** {N} plans`\n923\t\n924\t**Plan list** (always update):\n925\t```\n926\tPlans:\n927\t- [ ] {phase}-01-PLAN.md — {brief objective}\n928\t- [ ] {phase}-02-PLAN.md — {brief objective}\n929\t```\n930\t\n931\t4. Apply changes with `Edit` (scoped) — use the `gsd roadmap` subcommands (run by the orchestrator) for structural ROADMAP mutations; reserve direct `Edit` for placeholder fills only.\n932\t</step>\n933\t\n934\t<step name=\"git_commit\">\n935\t```bash\n936\tgsd_run query commit \"docs($PHASE): create phase plan\" --files \\\n937\t  .planning/phases/$PHASE-*/$PHASE-*-PLAN.md .planning/ROADMAP.md\n938\t```\n939\t</step>\n940\t\n941\t<step name=\"offer_next\">\n942\tReturn structured planning outcome to orchestrator.\n943\t</step>\n944\t\n945\t</execution_flow>\n946\t\n947\t<structured_returns>\n948\t\n949\tSee @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-guidance.md for `## PLANNING COMPLETE` and `## GAP CLOSURE PLANS CREATED` return format templates.\n950\t\n951\tSee @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-chunked.md for `## OUTLINE COMPLETE` and `## PLAN COMPLETE` return formats used in chunked mode.\n952\t\n953\t</structured_returns>\n954\t\n955\t<critical_rules>\n956\t\n957\t- **No re-reads:** Never re-read a range already in context. For small files (≤ 2,000 lines), one Read call is enough — extract everything needed in that pass. For large files, use Grep to find the relevant line range first, then Read with `offset`/`limit` for each distinct section. Duplicate range reads are forbidden.\n958\t- **Codebase pattern reads (Level 1+):** Read each source file once. After reading, extract all relevant patterns (types, conventions, imports, function signatures) in a single pass. Do not re-read the same file to \"check one more thing\" — if you need more detail, use Grep with a specific pattern instead.\n959\t- **Stop on sufficient evidence:** Once you have enough pattern examples to write deterministic task descriptions, stop reading. There is no benefit to reading more analogs of the same pattern.\n960\t- **No heredoc writes:** Always use the Write or Edit tool, never `Bash(cat << 'EOF')`.\n961\t\n962\t</critical_rules>\n963\t\n964\t<success_criteria>\n965\t\n966\t## Standard Mode\n967\t\n968\tPhase planning complete when:\n969\t- [ ] STATE.md read, project history disposition (mitigate / accept / transfer)\n986\t- [ ] Every threat has a Severity (critical|high|medium|low)\n987\t- [ ] Mitigations reference specific implementation (not generic advice)\n988\t\n989\t## Gap Closure Mode\n990\t\n991\tPlanning complete when:\n992\t- [ ] VERIFICATION.md or UAT.md loaded and gaps parsed\n993\t- [ ] Existing SUMMARYs read for context\n994\t- [ ]
