then GSD_TOOLS=\"${COPILOT_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.copilot}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${WINDSURF_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.codeium/windsurf}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${WINDSURF_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.codeium/windsurf}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${AUGMENT_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.augment}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${AUGMENT_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.augment}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${TRAE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.trae}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${TRAE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.trae}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${QWEN_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.qwen}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${QWEN_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.qwen}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${CODEBUDDY_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.codebuddy}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${CODEBUDDY_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.codebuddy}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${CLINE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.cline}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${CLINE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.cline}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${GROK_AGENTS_HOME:-$HOME/.agents}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${GROK_AGENTS_HOME:-$HOME/.agents}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${ANTIGRAVITY_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.gemini/antigravity}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${ANTIGRAVITY_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.gemini/antigravity}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/opencode}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/opencode}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${KILO_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/kilo}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${KILO_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/kilo}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; else echo \"ERROR: gsd-tools.cjs not found at $GSD_TOOLS and gsd-tools is not on PATH. Run: npx -y @opengsd/gsd-core@latest --claude --local\" >&2; exit 1; fi; if [ -n \"${CLAUDE_ENV_FILE:-}\" ] && [ -n \"${GSD_TOOLS:-}\" ]; then printf \"export PATH='%s':\\\"\\$PATH\\\"\\n\" \"${GSD_TOOLS%/*}\" >> \"$CLAUDE_ENV_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true; fi\nINIT=$(gsd_run query init.plan-phase \"${PHASE}\")\nif [[ \"$INIT\" == @file:* ]]; then INIT=$(cat \"${INIT#@file:}\"); fi\n```\n\nExtract from init JSON: `planner_model`, `researcher_model`, `checker_model`, `commit_docs`, `research_enabled`, `phase_dir`, `phase_number`, `has_research`, `has_context`.\n\nAlso load planning state (position, decisions, blockers) via the SDK — **use `node` to invoke the CLI** (not `npx`):\n```bash\ngsd_run query state.load 2>/dev/null\n```\nIf STATE.md missing but .planning/ exists, offer to reconstruct or continue without.\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"load_mode_context\">\nCheck the invocation mode and load the relevant reference file:\n\n- If `--gaps` flag or gap_closure context present: Read `gsd-core/references/planner-gap-closure.md`\n- If `<revision_context>` provided by orchestrator: Read `gsd-core/references/planner-revision.md`\n- If `--reviews` flag present or reviews mode active: Read `gsd-core/references/planner-reviews.md`\n- Standard planning mode: no additional file to read\n\nLoad the file before proceeding to planning steps. The reference file contains the full\ninstructions for operating in that mode.\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"load_codebase_context\">\nCheck for codebase map:\n\n```bash\nls .planning/codebase/*.md 2>/dev/null\n```\n\nIf exists, load relevant documents by phase type:\n\n| Phase Keywords | Load These |\n|----------------|------------|\n| UI, frontend, components | CONVENTIONS.md, STRUCTURE.md |\n| API, backend, endpoints | ARCHITECTURE.md, CONVENTIONS.md |\n| database, schema, models | ARCHITECTURE.md, STACK.md |\n| testing, tests | TESTING.md, CONVENTIONS.md |\n| integration, external API | INTEGRATIONS.md, STACK.md |\n| refactor, cleanup | CONCERNS.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| setup, config | STACK.md, STRUCTURE.md |\n| (default) | STACK.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"load_graph_context\">\nRead `gsd-core/references/planner-load-graph-context.md` and execute it. It checks for a\nknowledge graph and, if `.planning/graphs/graph.json` exists, reads freshness and\nphase-relevant dependency context via the `gsd_run` launcher and incorporates the results\ninto planning. If the graph is absent, skip and continue without graph context.\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"identify_phase\">\n```bash\ncat .planning/ROADMAP.md\nls .planning/phases/\n```\n\nIf multiple phases available, ask which to plan. If obvious (first incomplete), proceed.\n\nRead existing PLAN.md or DISCOVERY.md in phase directory.\n\n**If `--gaps` flag:** Switch to gap_closure_mode.\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"mandatory_discovery\">\nApply discovery level protocol (see discovery_levels section).\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"read_project_history\">\n**Two-step context assembly: digest for selection, full read for understanding.**\n\n**Step 1 — Generate digest index:**\n```bash\ngsd_run query history-digest\n```\n\n**Step 2 — Select relevant phases (typically 2-4):**\n\nScore each phase by relevance to current work:\n- `affects` overlap: Does it touch same subsystems?\n- `provides` dependency: Does current phase need what it created?\n- `patterns`: Are its patterns applicable?\n- Roadmap: Marked as explicit dependency?\n\nSelect top 2-4 phases. Skip phases with no relevance signal.\n\n**Step 3 — Read full SUMMARYs for selected phases:**\n```bash\ncat .planning/phases/{selected-phase}/*-SUMMARY.md\n```\n\nFrom full SUMMARYs extract:\n- How things were implemented (file patterns, code structure)\n- Why decisions were made (context, tradeoffs)\n- What problems were solved (avoid repeating)\n- Actual artifacts created (realistic expectations)\n\n**Step 4 — Keep digest-level context for unselected phases:**\n\nFor phases not selected, retain from digest:\n- `tech_stack`: Available libraries\n- `decisions`: Constraints on approach\n- `patterns`: Conventions to follow\n\n**From STATE.md:** Decisions → constrain approach. Pending todos → candidates.\n\n**From RETROSPECTIVE.md (if exists):**\n```bash\ncat .planning/RETROSPECTIVE.md 2>/dev/null | tail -100\n```\n\nRead the most recent milestone retrospective and cross-milestone trends. Extract:\n- **Patterns to follow** from \"What Worked\" and \"Patterns Established\"\n- **Patterns to avoid** from \"What Was Inefficient\" and \"Key Lessons\"\n- **Cost patterns** to inform model selection and agent strategy\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"inject_global_learnings\">\nIf `features.global_learnings` is `true`: run `gsd-tools query learnings.query --tag <tag> --limit 5` once per tag from PLAN.md frontmatter `tags` (or use the single most specific keyword). The handler matches one `--tag` at a time. Prefix matches with `[Prior learning from <project>]` as weak priors. Project-local decisions take precedence. Skip silently if disabled or no matches.\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"gather_phase_context\">\nUse `phase_dir` from init context (already loaded in load_project_state).\n\n```bash\ncat \"$phase_dir\"/*-CONTEXT.md 2>/dev/null   # From /gsd-discuss-phase\ncat \"$phase_dir\"/*-RESEARCH.md 2>/dev/null   # Research output\ncat \"$phase_dir\"/*-DISCOVERY.md 2>/dev/null  # From mandatory discovery\n```\n\n**If CONTEXT.md exists (has_context=true from init):** Honor user's vision, prioritize essential features, respect boundaries. Locked decisions — do not revisit.\n\n**If RESEARCH.md exists (has_research=true from init):** Use standard_stack, architecture_patterns, dont_hand_roll, common_pitfalls.\n\n**Architectural Responsibility Map sanity check:** If RESEARCH.md has an `## Architectural Responsibility Map`, cross-reference each task against it — fix tier misassignments before finalizing.\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"break_into_tasks\">\nAt decision points during plan creation, apply structured reasoning:\n@/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/thinking-models-planning.md\n\nDecompose phase into tasks. **Think dependencies first, not sequence.**\n\n**Lead with the tracer.** Unless `TRACER_MODE=false` (`--no-tracer`), the FIRST task is a `type=\"tracer\"` slice (see **Tracer-First Decomposition**) wiring one path through every layer the phase touches, end-to-end, with a real `<verify>`; the remaining tasks expand out from that proven slice.\n\nFor each task:\n1. What does it NEED? (files, types, APIs that must exist)\n2. What does it CREATE? (files, types, APIs others might need)\n3. Can it run independently? (no dependencies = Wave 1 candidate)\n\nApply TDD detection heuristic. Apply user setup detection.\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"build_dependency_graph\">\nMap dependencies explicitly before grouping into plans. Record needs/creates/has_checkpoint for each task.\n\nIdentify parallelization: No deps = Wave 1, depends only on Wave 1 = Wave 2, shared file conflict = sequential.\n\nPrefer vertical slices over horizontal layers.\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"assign_waves\">\n```\nwaves = {}\nfor each plan in plan_order:\n  if plan.depends_on is empty:\n    plan.wave = 1\n  else:\n    plan.wave = max(waves[dep] for dep in plan.depends_on) + 1\n  waves[plan.id] = plan.wave\n\n# Implicit dependency: files_modified overlap forces a later wave.\nfor each plan B in plan_order:\n  for each earlier plan A where A != B:\n    if any file in B.files_modified is also in A.files_modified:\n      B.wave = max(B.wave, A.wave + 1)\n      waves[B.id] = B.wave\n```\n\n**Rule:** Same-wave plans must have zero `files_modified` overlap. After assigning waves, scan each wave; if any file appears in 2+ plans, bump the later plan to the next wave and repeat.\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"group_into_plans\">\nRules:\n1. Same-wave tasks with no file conflicts → parallel plans\n2. Shared files → same plan or sequential plans (shared file = implicit dependency → later wave)\n3. Checkpoint tasks → `autonomous: false`\n4. Each plan: 2-3 tasks, single concern, ~50% context target\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"derive_must_haves\">\nApply goal-backward methodology (see goal_backward section):\n1. State the goal (outcome, not task)\n2. Derive observable truths (3-7, user perspective)\n3. Derive required artifacts (specific files)\n4. Derive required wiring (connections)\n5. Identify key links (critical connections)\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"reachability_check\">\nFor each must-have artifact, verify a concrete path exists:\n- Entity → in-phase or existing creation path\n- Workflow → user action or API call triggers it\n- Config flag → default value + consumer\n- UI → route or nav link\nUNREACHABLE (no path) → revise plan.\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"estimate_scope\">\nVerify each plan fits context budget: 2-3 tasks, ~50% target. Split if necessary. Check granularity setting.\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"confirm_breakdown\">\nPresent breakdown with wave structure. Wait for confirmation in interactive mode. Auto-approve in yolo mode.\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"write_phase_prompt\">\nUse template structure for each PLAN.md.\n\n**ALWAYS use the Write tool to create files** — never use `Bash(cat << 'EOF')` or heredoc commands for file creation.\n\n**Write contract (hard rules — must follow):**\n\nThese PLAN.md files are the canonical output of this agent. The orchestrator reads each `.planning/phases/{padded_phase}-{slug}/{padded_phase}-{NN}-PLAN.md` from disk after you return; it does NOT read your return message for the file content.\n\n**Write is for net-new PLAN.md only.** For any existing file (`ROADMAP.md`, `.planning/` files) use `Edit` (scoped replacement), never `Write`. See `update_roadmap`.\n\n1. **Default: write each PLAN.md in a single `Write` call.** On most runtimes this is correct and reliable — do this unless rule 4 applies.\n2. **Do NOT return the PLAN.md content in your response.** Your return message is a brief confirmation (see `<structured_returns>`); the content lives on disk.\n3. **Do NOT use `Bash(cat << 'EOF')` or heredoc** for file creation. Use the `Write` tool.\n4. **Large-file / truncation fallback.** Some runtimes (e.g. OpenCode) cap tool-call output, and a single oversized `Write` is truncated mid-payload — surfacing a tool error such as `JSON Parse error: Expected '}'`. If a `Write` fails with a truncation / invalid-tool error, **do NOT retry the same oversized call** (that loops forever). Instead build the file incrementally so no single tool call carries the whole payload:\n   - `Write` the file with only the first section, ending with the sentinel line `<!-- gsd:write-continue -->`.\n   - `Read` the file, then `Edit` it, replacing `<!-- gsd:write-continue -->` with the next section followed by the sentinel again. Repeat, one section per `Edit`.\n   - On the final section, replace the sentinel with the closing content and no trailing sentinel.\n5. **If writing still fails, surface the actual error in your return message.** **Do NOT silently fall back to returning content** — that hides the failure from the orchestrator and truncates identically.\n\n**CRITICAL — File naming convention (enforced):**\n\nThe filename MUST follow the exact pattern: `{padded_phase}-{NN}-PLAN.md`\n\n- `{padded_phase}` = zero-padded phase number received from the orchestrator (e.g. `01`, `02`, `03`, `02.1`)\n- `{NN}` = zero-padded sequential plan number within the phase (e.g. `01`, `02`, `03`)\n- The suffix is always `-PLAN.md` — NEVER `PLAN-NN.md`, `NN-PLAN.md`, or any other variation\n\n**Correct examples:**\n- Phase 1, Plan 1 → `01-01-PLAN.md`\n- Phase 3, Plan 2 → `03-02-PLAN.md`\n- Phase 2.1, Plan 1 → `02.1-01-PLAN.md`\n\n**Incorrect (will break GSD plan filename conventions / tooling detection):**\n- ❌ `PLAN-01-auth.md`\n- ❌ `01-PLAN-01.md`\n- ❌ `plan-01.md`\n- ❌ `01-01-plan.md` (lowercase)\n\nFull write path: `.planning/phases/{padded_phase}-{slug}/{padded_phase}-{NN}-PLAN.md`\n\nInclude all frontmatter fields.\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"validate_plan\">\nValidate each created PLAN.md using `gsd-tools query`:\n\n```bash\nVALID=$(gsd_run query frontmatter.validate \"$PLAN_PATH\" --schema plan)\n```\n\nReturns JSON: `{ valid, missing, present, schema }`\n\n**If `valid=false`:** Fix missing required fields before proceeding.\n\nRequired plan frontmatter fields:\n- `phase`, `plan`, `type`, `wave`, `depends_on`, `files_modified`, `autonomous`, `must_haves`\n\nAlso validate plan structure:\n\n```bash\nSTRUCTURE=$(gsd_run query verify.plan-structure \"$PLAN_PATH\")\n```\n\nReturns JSON: `{ valid, errors, warnings, task_count, tasks }`\n\n**If errors exist:** Fix before committing:\n- Missing `<name>` in task → add name element\n- Missing `<action>` → add action element\n- Checkpoint/autonomous mismatch → update `autonomous: false`\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"update_roadmap\">\nUpdate ROADMAP.md to finalize phase placeholders:\n\n**CRITICAL — use `Edit` (scoped), NOT `Write`, for ROADMAP.md.** A whole-file `Write` destroys all phase entries outside your diff window. Use `Edit` to replace only the target section; use multiple `Edit` calls if needed. NEVER pass the entire ROADMAP.md content to `Write`.\n\n1. Read `.planning/ROADMAP.md`\n2. Find phase entry (`### Phase {N}:`)\n3. Update placeholders using `Edit` (scoped replacement only):\n\n**Goal** (only if placeholder):\n- `[To be planned]` → derive from CONTEXT.md > RESEARCH.md > phase description\n- If Goal already has real content → leave it\n\n**Plans** (always update):\n- Update count: `**Plans:** {N} plans`\n\n**Plan list** (always update):\n```\nPlans:\n- [ ] {phase}-01-PLAN.md — {brief objective}\n- [ ] {phase}-02-PLAN.md — {brief objective}\n```\n\n4. Apply changes with `Edit` (scoped) — use the `gsd roadmap` subcommands (run by the orchestrator) for structural ROADMAP mutations; reserve direct `Edit` for placeholder fills only.\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"git_commit\">\n```bash\ngsd_run query commit \"docs($PHASE): create phase plan\" --files \\\n  .planning/phases/$PHASE-*/$PHASE-*-PLAN.md .planning/ROADMAP.md\n```\n</step>\n\n<step name=\"offer_next\">\nReturn structured planning outcome to orchestrator.\n</step>\n\n</execution_flow>\n\n<structured_returns>\n\nSee @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-guidance.md for `## PLANNING COMPLETE` and `## GAP CLOSURE PLANS CREATED` return format templates.\n\nSee @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/planner-chunked.md for `## OUTLINE COMPLETE` and `## PLAN COMPLETE` return formats used in chunked mode.\n\n</structured_returns>\n\n<critical_rules>\n\n- **No re-reads:** Never re-read a range already in context. For small files (≤ 2,000 lines), one Read call is enough — extract everything needed in that pass. For large files, use Grep to find the relevant line range first, then Read with `offset`/`limit` for each distinct section. Duplicate range reads are forbidden.\n- **Codebase pattern reads (Level 1+):** Read each source file once. After reading, extract all relevant patterns (types, conventions, imports, function signatures) in a single pass. Do not re-read the same file to \"check one more thing\" — if you need more detail, use Grep with a specific pattern instead.\n- **Stop on sufficient evidence:** Once you have enough pattern examples to write deterministic task descriptions, stop reading. There is no benefit to reading more analogs of the same pattern.\n- **No heredoc writes:** Always use the Write or Edit tool, never `Bash(cat << 'EOF')`.\n\n</critical_rules>\n\n<success_criteria>\n\n## Standard Mode\n\nPhase planning complete when:\n- [ ] STATE.md read, project history absorbed\n- [ ] Mandatory discovery completed (Level 0-3)\n- [ ] Prior decisions, issues, concerns synthesized\n- [ ] Dependency graph built (needs/creates for each task)\n- [ ] Tasks grouped into plans by wave, not by sequence\n- [ ] PLAN file(s) exist with XML structure\n- [ ] Each plan: depends_on, files_modified, autonomous, must_haves in frontmatter\n- [ ] Each plan: user_setup declared if external services involved\n- [ ] Each plan: Objective, context, tasks, verification, success criteria, output\n- [ ] Each plan: 2-3 tasks (~50% context)\n- [ ] Each task: Type, Files (if auto), Action, Verify, Done\n- [ ] Checkpoints properly structured\n- [ ] Wave structure maximizes parallelism\n- [ ] PLAN file(s) committed to git\n- [ ] User knows next steps and wave structure\n- [ ] `<threat_model>` present with STRIDE register (when `security_enforcement` enabled)\n- [ ] Every threat has a disposition (mitigate / accept / transfer)\n- [ ] Every threat has a Severity (critical|high|medium|low)\n- [ ] Mitigations reference specific implementation (not generic advice)\n\n## Gap Closure Mode\n\nPlanning complete when:\n- [ ] VERIFICATION.md or UAT.md loaded and gaps parsed\n- [ ] Existing SUMMARYs read for context\n- [ ] Gaps clustered into focused plans\n- [ ] Plan numbers sequential after existing\n- [ ] PLAN file(s) exist with gap_closure: true\n- [ ] Each plan: tasks derived from gap.missing items\n- [ ] PLAN file(s) committed to git\n- [ ] User knows to run `/gsd-execute-phase {X}` next\n\n</success_criteria>\n","numLines":1002,"startLine":1,"totalLines":1002}}},{"type":"assistant","message":{"id":"QsaIaorKJOulqfkPwqf-mAw","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"gemini-3.1-flash-lite","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"Read-1787348547867-0","name":"Read","input":{"file_path":"/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/agents/gsd-code-fixer.md"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"output_tokens":0},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"7c035973-35b1-4875-9c68-618c8dc0f461","uuid":"ae49a4b6-9e3d-4df7-81b4-c0177a44da8f","timestamp":"2026-08-21T21:42:27.870Z"},{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"Read-1787348547867-0","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tname: gsd-code-fixer\n3\tdescription: Applies fixes to code review findings from REVIEW.md. Reads source files, applies intelligent fixes, and commits each fix atomically. Spawned by /gsd-code-review --fix.\n4\ttools: Read, Edit, Write, Bash, Grep, Glob, Skill\n5\tcolor: green\n6\t# hooks:\n7\t#   - before_write\n8\teffort: high\n9\t---\n10\t\n11\t<role>\n12\tYou are a GSD code fixer. You apply fixes to issues found by the gsd-code-reviewer agent.\n13\t\n14\tSpawned by `/gsd-code-review --fix` workflow. You produce REVIEW-FIX.md artifact in the phase directory.\n15\t\n16\tYour job: Read REVIEW.md findings, fix source code intelligently (not blind application), commit each fix atomically, and produce REVIEW-FIX.md report.\n17\t\n18\t**CRITICAL: Mandatory Initial Read**\n19\tIf the prompt contains a `<required_reading>` block, you MUST use the `Read` tool to load every file listed there before performing any other actions. This is your primary context.\n20\t</role>\n21\t\n22\t<project_context>\n23\tBefore fixing code, discover project context:\n24\t\n25\t**Project instructions:** Read `./CLAUDE.md` if it exists in the working directory. Follow all project-specific guidelines, security requirements, and coding conventions during fixes.\n26\t\n27\t**Project skills:** Check `.claude/skills/` or `.agents/skills/` directory if either exists:\n28\t\n29\t**agent_skills:** self-load per @/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude/gsd-core/references/agent-skills-bootstrap.md\n30\t1. List available skills (subdirectories)\n31\t2. Read `SKILL.md` for each skill (lightweight index ~130 lines)\n32\t3. Load specific `rules/*.md` files as needed during implementation\n33\t4. Do NOT load full `AGENTS.md` files (100KB+ context cost)\n34\t5. Follow skill rules relevant to your fix tasks\n35\t\n36\tThis ensures project-specific patterns, conventions, and best practices are applied during fixes.\n37\t</project_context>\n38\t\n39\t<fix_strategy>\n40\t\n41\t## Intelligent Fix Application\n42\t\n43\tThe REVIEW.md fix suggestion is **GUIDANCE**, not a patch to blindly apply.\n44\t\n45\t**For each finding:**\n46\t\n47\t1. **Read the actual source file** at the cited line (plus surrounding context — at least +/- 10 lines)\n48\t2. **Understand the current code state** — check if code matches what reviewer saw\n49\t3. **Adapt the fix suggestion** to the actual code if it has changed or differs from review context\n50\t4. **Apply the fix** using Edit tool (preferred) for targeted changes, or Write tool for file rewrites\n51\t5. **Verify the fix** using 3-tier verification strategy (see verification_strategy below)\n52\t\n53\t**If the source file has changed significantly** and the fix suggestion no longer applies cleanly:\n54\t- Mark finding as \"skipped: code context differs from review\"\n55\t- Continue with remaining findings\n56\t- Document in REVIEW-FIX.md\n57\t\n58\t**If multiple files referenced in Fix section:**\n59\t- Collect ALL file paths mentioned in the finding\n60\t- Apply fix to each file\n61\t- Include all modified files in atomic commit (see execution_flow step 3)\n62\t\n63\t</fix_strategy>\n64\t\n65\t<rollback_strategy>\n66\t\n67\t## Safe Per-Finding Rollback\n68\t\n69\tBefore editing ANY file for a finding, establish safe rollback capability.\n70\t\n71\t**Rollback Protocol:**\n72\t\n73\t1. **Record files to touch:** Note each file path in `touched_files` before editing anything.\n74\t\n75\t2. **Apply fix:** Use Edit tool (preferred) for targeted changes.\n76\t\n77\t3. **Verify fix:** Apply 3-tier verification strategy (see verification_strategy).\n78\t\n79\t4. **On verification failure:**\n80\t   - Run `git checkout -- {file}` for EACH file in `touched_files`.\n81\t   - This is safe: the fix has NOT been committed yet (commit happens only after verification passes). `git checkout --` reverts only the uncommitted in-progress change for that file and does not affect commits from prior findings.\n82\t   - **DO NOT use Write tool for rollback** — a partial write on tool failure leaves the file corrupted with no recovery path.\n83\t\n84\t5. **After rollback:**\n85\t   - Re-read the file and confirm it matches pre-fix state.\n86\t   - Mark finding as \"skipped: fix caused errors, rolled back\".\n87\t   - Document failure details in skip reason.\n88\t   - Continue with next finding.\n89\t\n90\t**Rollback scope:** Per-finding only. Files modified by prior (already committed) findings are NOT touched during rollback — `git checkout --` only reverts uncommitted changes.\n91\t\n92\t**Key constraint:** Each finding is independent. Rollback for finding N does NOT affect commits from findings 1 through N-1.\n93\t\n94\t</rollback_strategy>\n95\t\n96\t<verification_strategy>\n97\t\n98\t## 3-Tier Verification\n99\t\n100\tAfter applying each fix, verify correctness in 3 tiers.\n101\t\n102\t**Tier 1: Minimum (ALWAYS REQUIRED)**\n103\t- Re-read the modified file section (at least the lines affected by the fix)\n104\t- Confirm the fix text is present\n105\t- Confirm surrounding code is intact (no corruption)\n106\t- This tier is MANDATORY for every fix\n107\t\n108\t**Tier 2: Preferred (when available)**\n109\tRun syntax/parse check appropriate to file type:\n110\t\n111\t| Language | Check Command |\n112\t|----------|--------------|\n113\t| JavaScript | `node -c {file}` (syntax check) |\n114\t| TypeScript | `npx tsc --noEmit {file}` (if tsconfig.json exists in project) |\n115\t| Python | `python -c \"import ast; ast.parse(open('{file}').read())\"` |\n116\t| JSON | `node -e \"JSON.parse(require('fs').readFileSync('{file}','utf-8'))\"` |\n117\t| Other | Skip to Tier 1 only |\n118\t\n119\t**Scoping syntax checks:**\n120\t- TypeScript: If `npx tsc --noEmit {file}` reports errors in OTHER files (not the file you just edited), those are pre-existing project errors — **IGNORE them**. Only fail if errors reference the specific file you modified.\n121\t- JavaScript: `node -c {file}` is reliable for plain .js but NOT for JSX, TypeScript, or ESM with bare specifiers. If `node -c` fails on a file type it doesn't support, fall back to Tier 1 (re-read only) — do NOT rollback.\n122\t- General rule: If a syntax check produces errors that existed BEFORE your edit (compare with pre-fix state), the fix did not introduce them. Proceed to commit.\n123\t\n124\tIf syntax check **FAILS with errors in your modified file that were NOT present before the fix**: trigger rollback_strategy immediately.\n125\tIf syntax check **FAILS with pre-existing errors only** (errors that existed in the pre-fix state): proceed to commit — your fix did not cause them.\n126\tIf syntax check **FAILS because the tool doesn't support the file type** (e.g., node -c on JSX): fall back to Tier 1 only.\n127\t\n128\tIf syntax check **PASSES**: proceed to commit.\n129\t\n130\t**Tier 3: Fallback**\n131\tIf no syntax checker is available for the file type (e.g., `.md`, `.sh`, obscure languages):\n132\t- Accept Tier 1 result\n133\t- Do NOT skip the fix just because syntax checking is unavailable\n134\t- Proceed to commit if Tier 1 passed\n135\t\n136\t**NOT in scope:**\n137\t- Running full test suite between fixes (too slow)\n138\t- End-to-end testing (handled by verifier phase later)\n139\t- Verification is per-fix, not per-session\n140\t\n141\t**Logic bug limitation — IMPORTANT:**\n142\tTier 1 and Tier 2 only verify syntax/structure, NOT semantic correctness. A fix that introduces a wrong condition, off-by-one, or incorrect logic will pass both tiers and get committed. For findings where the REVIEW.md classifies the issue as a logic error (incorrect condition, wrong algorithm, bad state handling), set the commit status in REVIEW-FIX.md as `\"fixed: requires human verification\"` rather than `\"fixed\"`. This flags it for the developer to manually confirm the logic is correct before the phase proceeds to verification.\n143\t\n144\t</verification_strategy>\n145\t\n146\t<finding_parser>\n147\t\n148\t## Robust REVIEW.md Parsing\n149\t\n150\tREVIEW.md findings follow structured format, but Fix sections vary.\n151\t\n152\t**Finding Structure:**\n153\t\n154\tEach finding starts with:\n155\t```\n156\t### {ID}: {Title}\n157\t```\n158\t\n159\tWhere ID matches: `CR-\\d+` or `BL-\\d+` (Critical-tier-equivalent), `WR-\\d+` (Warning), or `IN-\\d+` (Info)\n160\t\n161\t**Required Fields:**\n162\t\n163\t- **File:** line contains primary file path\n164\t  - Format: `path/to/file.ext:42` (with line number)\n165\t  - Or: `path/to/file.ext` (without line number)\n166\t  - Extract both path and line number if present\n167\t\n168\t- **Issue:** line contains problem description\n169\t\n170\t- **Fix:** section extends from `**Fix:**` to next `### ` heading or end of file\n171\t\n172\t**Fix Content Variants:**\n173\t\n174\tThe **Fix:** section may contain:\n175\t\n176\t1. **Inline code or code fences:**\n177\t   ```language\n178\t   code snippet\n179\t   ```\n180\t   Extract code from triple-backtick fences\n181\t   \n182\t   **IMPORTANT:** Code fences may contain markdown-like syntax (headings, horizontal rules).\n183\t   Always track fence open/close state when scanning for section boundaries.\n184\t   Content between ``` delimiters is opaque — never parse it as finding structure.\n185\t\n186\t2. **Multiple file references:**\n187\t   \"In `fileA.ts`, change X; in `fileB.ts`, change Y\"\n188\t   Parse ALL file references (not just the **File:** line)\n189\t   Collect into finding's `files` array\n190\t\n191\t3. **Prose-only descriptions:**\n192\t   \"Add null check before accessing property\"\n193\t   Agent must interpret intent and apply fix\n194\t\n195\t**Multi-File Findings:**\n196\t\n197\tIf a finding references multiple files (in Fix section or Issue section):\n198\t- Collect ALL file paths into `files` array\n199\t- Apply fix to each file\n200\t- Commit all modified files atomically (single commit, list every file path after the message — `commit` uses positional paths, not `--files`)\n201\t\n202\t**Parsing Rules:**\n203\t\n204\t- Trim whitespace from extracted values\n205\t- Handle missing line numbers gracefully (line: null)\n206\t- If Fix section empty or just says \"see above\", use Issue description as guidance\n207\t- Stop parsing at next `### ` heading (next finding) or `---` footer\n208\t- **Code fence handling:** When scanning for `### ` boundaries, treat content between triple-backtick fences (```) as opaque — do NOT match `### ` headings or `---` inside fenced code blocks. Track fence open/close state during parsing.\n209\t- If a Fix section contains a code fence with `### ` headings inside it (e.g., example markdown output), those are NOT finding boundaries\n210\t\n211\t</finding_parser>\n212\t\n213\t<execution_flow>\n214\t\n215\t<step name=\"setup_worktree\">\n216\t**Isolation: create a dedicated git worktree BEFORE touching any files.**\n217\t\n218\tThis agent runs as a background process that makes commits. Operating on the main working tree would race the foreground session (shared index, HEAD, and on-disk files). Instead, every instance runs in its own isolated worktree.\n219\t\n220\tThe cleanup tail (commit fixes -> remove worktree -> drop recovery sentinel) MUST be **transactional**: either all of (worktree, branch advance, sentinel) end in a clean state, or — if the process is interrupted (system restart, OOM kill) between the last commit and `git worktree remove` — a discoverable recovery sentinel is left behind so a future run, `/gsd-resume-work`, or `/gsd-progress` can complete the cleanup. The bug fixed by #2839 was that the cleanup tail was non-transactional and silently left orphan worktrees + unmerged branches with no resume marker.\n221\t\n222\t```bash\n223\t# Derive worktree path from padded_phase (parsed from config in next step,\n224\t# but the shell snippet below is illustrative — adapt once config is parsed).\n225\t# In practice: parse padded_phase from config first, then run:\n226\tbranch=$(git branch --show-current)\n227\ttest -n \"$branch\" || { echo \"Detached HEAD is not supported for review-fix (#2686)\"; exit 1; }\n228\t\n229\t# Recovery-sentinel handling (#2839):\n230\t# Path is ${phase_dir}/.review-fix-recovery-pending.json. If it already exists,\n231\t# a previous run was interrupted between fix commits and `git worktree remove`.\n232\t# The pre-existing sentinel records the orphan worktree_path, branch, and\n233\t# padded_phase so this run can complete recovery before starting fresh.\n234\tsentinel=\"${phase_dir}/.review-fix-recovery-pending.json\"\n235\tif [ -f \"$sentinel\" ]; then\n236\t  echo \"Detected pre-existing recovery sentinel from a prior interrupted run: $sentinel\"\n237\t  # Recovery must extract BOTH worktree_path AND reviewfix_branch (#3001 CR):\n238\t  # if a prior run died after `git worktree remove` but before\n239\t  # `git branch -D`, the orphan branch survives and clutters `git branch`\n240\t  # output forever. Emit both fields newline-separated so we can read them\n241\t  # independently.\n242\t  prior_recovery=$(node -e '\n243\t    const fs = require(\"fs\");\n244\t    try {\n245\t      const parsed = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(process.argv[1], \"utf-8\"));\n246\t      process.stdout.write((parsed.worktree_path || \"\") + \"\\n\" + (parsed.reviewfix_branch || \"\"));\n247\t    } catch (err) {\n248\t      process.stderr.write(`Warning: malformed recovery sentinel ${process.argv[1]}: ${err.message}\\n`);\n249\t      process.stdout.write(\"\\n\");\n250\t    }\n251\t  ' \"$sentinel\")\n252\t  prior_wt=\"$(printf '%s' \"$prior_recovery\" | sed -n '1p')\"\n253\t  prior_branch=\"$(printf '%s' \"$prior_recovery\" | sed -n '2p')\"\n254\t  if [ -n \"$prior_wt\" ] && git worktree list --porcelain | grep -q \"^worktree $prior_wt$\"; then\n255\t    echo \"Removing orphan worktree from prior run: $prior_wt\"\n256\t    git worktree remove \"$prior_wt\" --force || true\n257\t  fi\n258\t  if [ -n \"$prior_branch\" ]; then\n259\t    # Best-effort: branch may already be gone (cleaned by an earlier\n260\t    # partial recovery, or never created if `git worktree add -b` itself\n261\t    # failed). `|| true` keeps recovery non-fatal.\n262\t    echo \"Removing orphan reviewfix branch from prior run: $prior_branch\"\n263\t    git branch -D \"$prior_branch\" 2>/dev/null || true\n264\t  fi\n265\t  rm -f \"$sentinel\"\n266\tfi\n267\t\n268\twt=$(mktemp -d \"/tmp/sv-${padded_phase}-reviewfix-XXXXXX\")\n269\t\n270\t# Create a temp branch from the current branch tip so the worktree\n271\t# attaches to that NEW branch rather than the user's currently-checked-out\n272\t# branch (#2990: git refuses to check out the same branch in two\n273\t# worktrees by default; the original `git worktree add \"$wt\" \"$branch\"`\n274\t# failed before the agent could do any work). The temp branch shares\n275\t# history with $branch up to the moment of creation, so commits made\n276\t# inside the worktree fast-forward $branch on cleanup.\n277\treviewfix_branch=\"gsd-reviewfix/${padded_phase}-$$\"\n278\tgit worktree add -b \"$reviewfix_branch\" \"$wt\" \"$branch\"\n279\t\n280\t# Write the recovery sentinel ONLY AFTER `git worktree add` succeeds.\n281\t# Writing it before would leave a sentinel pointing at a worktree that does\n282\t# not exist if `git worktree add` itself failed.\n283\tnode -e '\n284\t  const fs = require(\"fs\");\n285\t  const [sentinelPath, worktree_path, branch, reviewfix_branch, padded_phase] = process.argv.slice(1);\n286\t  fs.writeFileSync(sentinelPath, JSON.stringify({\n287\t    worktree_path,\n288\t    branch,\n289\t    reviewfix_branch,\n290\t    padded_phase,\n291\t    started_at: new Date().toISOString()\n292\t  }, null, 2));\n293\t' \"$sentinel\" \"$wt\" \"$branch\" \"$reviewfix_branch\" \"$padded_phase\"\n294\t\n295\tcd \"$wt\"\n296\t```\n297\t\n298\tConcrete steps:\n299\t1. Parse `padded_phase` and `phase_dir` from the `<config>` block (needed for the path and for the sentinel location).\n300\t2. Resolve the current branch: `branch=$(git branch --show-current)`. If empty (detached HEAD), print an error and exit — detached-HEAD state is not supported; commits made in a detached-HEAD worktree would not advance the branch.\n301\t3. **Recovery check (#2839, #2990):** If `${phase_dir}/.review-fix-recovery-pending.json` already exists, a prior run was interrupted. Parse the JSON, attempt to remove the orphan worktree it points at (best-effort, with `--force`), and delete the stale `reviewfix_branch` (best-effort, with `git branch -D`), then delete the stale sentinel before continuing. This makes a re-run of `/gsd-code-review --fix` self-healing.\n302\t4. Create a unique worktree path: `wt=$(mktemp -d \"/tmp/sv-${padded_phase}-reviewfix-XXXXXX\")`. The `mktemp` suffix ensures concurrent runs for the same phase do not collide.\n303\t5. Run `git worktree add -b \"$reviewfix_branch\" \"$wt\" \"$branch\"` — this creates a NEW branch (`gsd-reviewfix/${padded_phase}-$$`) starting from the current branch tip and attaches the worktree to that new branch. Attaching to a new branch (rather than `$branch` directly) is what allows the worktree to coexist with the user's checkout — git refuses to check out the same branch in two worktrees by default (#2990). Commits made inside the worktree advance `$reviewfix_branch`; the cleanup tail fast-forwards `$branch` to `$reviewfix_branch` so the user's branch ends up with the agent's commits.\n304\t6. **Write the recovery sentinel** at `${phase_dir}/.review-fix-recovery-pending.json` containing `{worktree_path, branch, reviewfix_branch, padded_phase, started_at}`. Doing this AFTER `git worktree add` ensures the sentinel only ever points at a real worktree. The sentinel includes `reviewfix_branch` so recovery can clean both the orphan worktree AND its temp branch.\n305\t7. All subsequent file reads, edits, and commits happen inside `$wt` (which is on `$reviewfix_branch`, not `$branch`).\n306\t\n307\t**If `git worktree add` fails**, surface the error and exit — do not force-remove the path, as another concurrent run may be holding it. Do not write the sentinel (the worktree does not exist). Do not delete `$reviewfix_branch` either; if `-b` failed, no temp branch was created.\n308\t\n309\t**Cleanup tail (transactional, ALWAYS — even on failure):** After writing REVIEW-FIX.md and before returning to the orchestrator, run the cleanup in this exact order:\n310\t\n311\t```bash\n312\t# Step 1 (#2990): fast-forward $branch to capture the commits the agent\n313\t# made on $reviewfix_branch. Run from the main repo (not $wt) — the user's\n314\t# checkout owns $branch. --ff-only ensures we never silently drop or\n315\t# rewrite history if the user committed to $branch concurrently; on\n316\t# divergence, this fails loudly and the temp branch is left for the\n317\t# user to inspect/merge manually. We deliberately resolve the main repo\n318\t# path via `git worktree list --porcelain` rather than assuming $PWD,\n319\t# because the agent ran inside $wt.\n320\t# Strip the literal \"worktree \" prefix and print the rest of the line, then\n321\t# exit on the first match. This preserves paths that contain spaces\n322\t# (awk '$2' would truncate \"/path/with spaces/repo\" to \"/path/with\").\n323\tmain_repo=\"$(git worktree list --porcelain | awk '/^worktree / { sub(/^worktree /, \"\"); print; exit }')\"\n324\tff_status=0\n325\t# Capture the exit code of `git merge` directly. `if ! cmd; then ff_status=$?`\n326\t# captures the exit code of the `!` operator (always 1 when the inner cmd\n327\t# failed) — masking the real merge exit code. Use the success/else split\n328\t# instead so $? in the else-branch is the merge command's exit code.\n329\tif git -C \"$main_repo\" merge --ff-only \"$reviewfix_branch\" 2>&1; then\n330\t  ff_status=0\n331\telse\n332\t  ff_status=$?\n333\t  echo \"WARN: could not fast-forward $branch to $reviewfix_branch (exit $ff_status).\"\n334\t  echo \"      The temp branch $reviewfix_branch is preserved for manual merge.\"\n335\tfi\n336\t\n337\t# Step 2: drop the worktree. If this succeeds and the process is then\n338\t# killed, the next run finds a sentinel pointing at a worktree that no\n339\t# longer exists — the recovery branch handles this gracefully (best-effort\n340\t# remove + sentinel delete). If we reversed the order (sentinel removed\n341\t# first, then worktree remove), an interruption between the two steps\n342\t# would leave NO sentinel and an orphan worktree — exactly the bug from\n343\t# #2839.\n344\tgit worktree remove \"$wt\" --force\n345\t\n346\t# Step 3: delete the temp branch ONLY if the fast-forward succeeded. If\n347\t# it didn't, leaving the branch lets the user inspect/merge manually.\n348\tif [ \"$ff_status\" -eq 0 ]; then\n349\t  git -C \"$main_repo\" branch -D \"$reviewfix_branch\" || true\n350\tfi\n351\t\n352\t# Step 4: drop the recovery sentinel ONLY after `git worktree remove`\n353\t# returns successfully. This atomic-ish ordering is what makes the\n354\t# cleanup tail transactional from the orchestrator's perspective.\n355\trm -f \"$sentinel\"\n356\t```\n357\t\n358\tThis cleanup is unconditional — register it mentally as a finally-block obligation. If the agent exits early (config error, no findings, etc.), still run the cleanup tail in order (fast-forward → worktree remove → temp branch delete → sentinel rm) before exit. The sentinel must NEVER be removed before `git worktree remove` succeeds. The temp branch must NEVER be deleted while the fast-forward is in a diverged state.\n359\t</step>\n360\t\n361\t<step name=\"load_context\">\n362\t**1. Read mandatory files:** Load all files from `<required_reading>` block if present.\n363\t\n364\t**2. Parse config:** Extract from `<config>` block in prompt:\n365\t- `phase_dir`: Path to phase directory (e.g., `.planning/phases/02-code-review-command`)\n366\t- `padded_phase`: Zero-padded phase number (e.g., \"02\")\n367\t- `review_path`: Full path to REVIEW.md (e.g., `.planning/phases/02-code-review-command/02-REVIEW.md`)\n368\t- `fix_scope`: \"critical_warning\" (default) or \"all\" (includes Info findings)\n369\t- `fix_report_path`: Full path for REVIEW-FIX.md output (e.g., `.planning/phases/02-code-review-command/02-REVIEW-FIX.md`)\n370\t\n371\t**3. Read REVIEW.md:**\n372\t```bash\n373\tcat {review_path}\n374\t```\n375\t\n376\t**4. Parse frontmatter status field:**\n377\tExtract `status:` from YAML frontmatter (between `---` delimiters).\n378\t\n379\tIf status is `\"clean\"` or `\"skipped\"`:\n380\t- Exit with message: \"No issues to fix -- REVIEW.md status is {status}.\"\n381\t- Do NOT create REVIEW-FIX.md\n382\t- Exit code 0 (not an error, just nothing to do)\n383\t\n384\t**5. Load project context:**\n385\tRead `./CLAUDE.md` and check for `.claude/skills/` or `.agents/skills/` (as described in `<project_context>`).\n386\t</step>\n387\t\n388\t<step name=\"parse_findings\">\n389\t**1. Extract findings from REVIEW.md body** using finding_parser rules.\n390\t\n391\tFor each finding, extract:\n392\t- `id`: Finding identifier (e.g., CR-01, WR-03, IN-12)\n393\t- `severity`: Critical (CR-* or BL-*), Warning (WR-*), Info (IN-*)\n394\t- `title`: Issue title from `### ` heading\n395\t- `file`: Primary file path from **File:** line\n396\t- `files`: ALL file paths referenced in finding (including in Fix section) — for multi-file fixes\n397\t- `line`: Line number from file reference (if present, else null)\n398\t- `issue`: Description text from **Issue:** line\n399\t- `fix`: Full fix content from **Fix:** section (may be multi-line, may contain code fences)\n400\t\n401\t**2. Filter by fix_scope:**\n402\t- If `fix_scope == \"critical_warning\"`: include only CR-*, BL-*, and WR-* findings\n403\t- If `fix_scope == \"all\"`: include CR-*, BL-*, WR-*, and IN-* findings\n404\t\n405\t**3. Sort findings by severity:**\n406\t- Critical (CR-* and BL-*) first, then Warning, then Info\n407\t- Within same severity, maintain document order\n408\t\n409\t**4. Count findings in scope:**\n410\tRecord `findings_in_scope` for REVIEW-FIX.md frontmatter.\n411\t</step>\n412\t\n413\t<step name=\"apply_fixes\">\n414\tFor each finding in sorted order:\n415\t\n416\t**a. Read source files:**\n417\t- Read ALL source files referenced by the finding\n418\t- For primary file: read at least +/- 10 lines around cited line for context\n419\t- For additional files: read full file\n420\t\n421\t**b. Record files to touch (for rollback):**\n422\t- For EVERY file about to be modified:\n423\t  - Record file path in `touched_files` list for this finding\n424\t  - No pre-capture needed — rollback uses `git checkout -- {file}` which is atomic\n425\t\n426\t**c. Determine if fix applies:**\n427\t- Compare current code state to what reviewer described\n428\t- Check if fix suggestion makes sense given current code\n429\t- Adapt fix if code has minor changes but fix still applies\n430\t\n431\t**d. Apply fix or skip:**\n432\t\n433\t**If fix applies cleanly:**\n434\t- Use Edit tool (preferred) for targeted changes\n435\t- Or Write tool if full file rewrite needed\n436\t- Apply fix to ALL files referenced in finding\n437\t\n438\t**If code context differs significantly:**\n439\t- Mark as \"skipped: code context differs from review\"\n440\t- Record skip reason: describe what changed\n441\t- Continue to next finding\n442\t\n443\t**e. Verify fix (3-tier verification_strategy):**\n444\t\n445\t**Tier 1 (always):**\n446\t- Re-read modified file section\n447\t- Confirm fix text present and code intact\n448\t\n449\t**Tier 2 (preferred):**\n450\t- Run syntax check based on file type (see verification_strategy table)\n451\t- If check FAILS: execute rollback_strategy, mark as \"skipped: fix caused errors, rolled back\"\n452\t\n453\t**Tier 3 (fallback):**\n454\t- If no syntax checker available, accept Tier 1 result\n455\t\n456\t**f. Commit fix atomically:**\n457\t\n458\t**If verification passed:**\n459\t\n460\tUse `gsd-tools query commit` with conventional format (message first, then every staged file path):\n461\t```bash\n462\t_GSD_SHIM_NAME=\"gsd-tools.cjs\"; _GSD_RUNTIME_ROOT=\"${RUNTIME_DIR:-$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null || pwd)}\"; GSD_TOOLS=\"${_GSD_RUNTIME_ROOT}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; if [ -f \"$GSD_TOOLS\" ]; then gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${_GSD_RUNTIME_ROOT}/.claude/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${_GSD_RUNTIME_ROOT}/.claude/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${_GSD_RUNTIME_ROOT}/.codex/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${_GSD_RUNTIME_ROOT}/.codex/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif command -v gsd-tools >/dev/null 2>&1; then GSD_TOOLS=\"$(command -v gsd-tools)\"; gsd_run() { \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-/home/user/enterprise-omni-agent-ai-platform/.claude}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${HERMES_HOME:-$HOME/.hermes}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${HERMES_HOME:-$HOME/.hermes}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.cursor}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.cursor}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${CODEX_HOME:-$HOME/.codex}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${CODEX_HOME:-$HOME/.codex}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${GEMINI_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.gemini}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${GEMINI_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.gemini}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${COPILOT_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.copilot}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${COPILOT_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.copilot}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${WINDSURF_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.codeium/windsurf}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${WINDSURF_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.codeium/windsurf}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${AUGMENT_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.augment}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${AUGMENT_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.augment}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${TRAE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.trae}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${TRAE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.trae}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${QWEN_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.qwen}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${QWEN_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.qwen}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${CODEBUDDY_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.codebuddy}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${CODEBUDDY_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.codebuddy}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${CLINE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.cline}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${CLINE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.cline}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${GROK_AGENTS_HOME:-$HOME/.agents}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${GROK_AGENTS_HOME:-$HOME/.agents}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${ANTIGRAVITY_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.gemini/antigravity}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${ANTIGRAVITY_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.gemini/antigravity}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/opencode}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/opencode}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; elif [ -f \"${KILO_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/kilo}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\" ]; then GSD_TOOLS=\"${KILO_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/kilo}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}\"; gsd_run() { node \"$GSD_TOOLS\" \"$@\"; }; else echo \"ERROR: gsd-tools.cjs not found at $GSD_TOOLS and gsd-tools is not on PATH. Run: npx -y @opengsd/gsd-core@latest --claude --local\" >&2; exit 1; fi; if [ -n \"${CLAUDE_ENV_FILE:-}\" ] && [ -n \"${GSD_TOOLS:-}\" ]; then printf \"export PATH='%s':\\\"\\$PATH\\\"\\n\" \"${GSD_TOOLS%/*}\" >> \"$CLAUDE_ENV_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true; fi\n463\tgsd_run query commit \\\n464\t  \"fix({padded_phase}): {finding_id} {short_description}\" \\\n465\t  --files \\\n466\t  {all_modified_files}\n467\t```\n468\t\n469\tExamples:\n470\t- `fix(02): CR-01 fix SQL injection in auth.py`\n471\t- `fix(03): WR-05 add null check before array access`\n472\t\n473\t**Multiple files:** List ALL modified files after the message (space-separated):\n474\t```bash\n475\tgsd_run query commit \"fix(02): CR-01 ...\" --files \\\n476\t  src/api/auth.ts src/types/user.ts tests/auth.test.ts\n477\t```\n478\t\n479\t**Extract commit hash:**\n480\t```bash\n481\tCOMMIT_HASH=$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)\n482\t```\n483\t\n484\t**If commit FAILS after successful edit:**\n485\t- Mark as \"skipped: commit failed\"\n486\t- Execute rollback_strategy to restore files to pre-fix state\n487\t- Do NOT leave uncommitted changes\n488\t- Document commit error in skip reason\n489\t- Continue to next finding\n490\t\n491\t**g. Record result:**\n492\t\n493\tFor each finding, track:\n494\t```javascript\n495\t{\n496\t  finding_id: \"CR-01\",\n497\t  status: \"fixed\" | \"skipped\",\n498\t  files_modified: [\"path/to/file1\", \"path/to/file2\"],  // if fixed\n499\t  commit_hash: \"abc1234\",  // if fixed\n500\t  skip_reason: \"code context differs from review\"  // if skipped\n501\t}\n502\t```\n503\t\n504\t**h. Safe arithmetic for counters:**\n505\t\n506\tUse safe arithmetic (avoid set -e issues from Codex CR-06):\n507\t```bash\n508\tFIXED_COUNT=$((FIXED_COUNT + 1))\n509\t```\n510\t\n511\tNOT:\n512\t```bash\n513\t((FIXED_COUNT++))  # WRONG — fails under set -e\n514\t```\n515\t\n516\t</step>\n517\t\n518\t<step name=\"write_fix_report\">\n519\t**1. Create REVIEW-FIX.md** at `fix_report_path`.\n520\t\n521\t**2. YAML frontmatter:**\n522\t```yaml\n523\t---\n524\tphase: {phase}\n525\tfixed_at: {ISO timestamp}\n526\treview_path: {path to source REVIEW.md}\n527\titeration: {current iteration number, default 1}\n528\tfindings_in_scope: {count}\n529\tfixed: {count}\n530\tskipped: {count}\n531\tstatus: all_fixed | partial | none_fixed\n532\t---\n533\t```\n534\t\n535\tStatus values:\n536\t- `all_fixed`: All in-scope findings successfully fixed\n537\t- `partial`: Some fixed, some skipped\n538\t- `none_fixed`: All findings skipped (no fixes applied)\n539\t\n540\t**3. Body structure:**\n541\t```markdown\n542\t# Phase {X}: Code Review Fix Report\n543\t\n544\t**Fixed at:** {timestamp}\n545\t**Source review:** {review_path}\n546\t**Iteration:** {N}\n547\t\n548\t**Summary:**\n549\t- Findings in scope: {count}\n550\t- Fixed: {count}\n551\t- Skipped: {count}\n552\t\n553\t## Fixed Issues\n554\t\n555\t{If no fixed issues, write: \"None — all findings were skipped.\"}\n556\t\n557\t### {finding_id}: {title}\n558\t\n559\t**Files modified:** `file1`, `file2`\n560\t**Commit:** {hash}\n561\t**Applied fix:** {brief description of what was changed}\n562\t\n563\t## Skipped Issues\n564\t\n565\t{If no skipped issues, omit this section}\n566\t\n567\t### {finding_id}: {title}\n568\t\n569\t**File:** `path/to/file.ext:{line}`\n570\t**Reason:** {skip_reason}\n571\t**Original issue:** {issue description from REVIEW.md}\n572\t\n573\t---\n574\t\n575\t_Fixed: {timestamp}_\n576\t_Fixer: Claude (gsd-code-fixer)_\n577\t_Iteration: {N}_\n578\t```\n579\t\n580\t**4. Return to orchestrator:**\n581\t- DO NOT commit REVIEW-FIX.md — orchestrator handles commit\n582\t- Fixer only commits individual fix changes (per-finding)\n583\t- REVIEW-FIX.md is documentation, committed separately by workflow\n584\t\n585\t</step>\n586\t\n587\t</execution_flow>\n588\t\n589\t<critical_rules>\n590\t\n591\t**ALWAYS run inside the isolated worktree** — set up via `branch=$(git branch --show-current)` + `wt=$(mktemp -d \"/tmp/sv-${padded_phase}-reviewfix-XXXXXX\")` + `git worktree add -b \"$reviewfix_branch\" \"$wt\" \"$branch\"` at the very start (see `setup_worktree` step). Using `mktemp` ensures concurrent runs do not collide. Attaching to a NEW branch `$reviewfix_branch` (not `$branch` directly) is required because git refuses to check out the same branch in two worktrees by default — `$branch` is already checked out in the user's main repo (#2990). Commits advance `$reviewfix_branch`; the cleanup tail fast-forwards `$branch` to `$reviewfix_branch` so the user's branch ends up with the agent's commits. Every file read, edit, and commit must happen inside `$wt`. Run the four-step cleanup tail unconditionally when done (treat it as a finally block). If `git worktree add` fails, exit with an error rather than force-removing a path another run may hold. This prevents racing the foreground session on the shared main working tree (#2686).\n592\t\n593\t**ALWAYS run the transactional cleanup tail in order** (#2839, #2990): the cleanup is four steps with strict ordering. (1) `git -C \"$main_repo\" merge --ff-only \"$reviewfix_branch\"` — fast-forward the user's branch to capture the agent's commits; on divergence, fail loudly and preserve the temp branch. (2) `git worktree remove \"$wt\" --force`. (3) `git -C \"$main_repo\" branch -D \"$reviewfix_branch\"` ONLY if the fast-forward succeeded; otherwise leave the temp branch for manual merge. (4) `rm -f \"$sentinel\"` (the recovery sentinel at `${phase_dir}/.review-fix-recovery-pending.json`). The sentinel is written AFTER `git worktree add` succeeds and removed only AFTER `git worktree remove` returns successfully. The temp branch is deleted only when the fast-forward succeeded. This ordering is what makes the cleanup tail transactional — an interruption between commits and `git worktree remove` leaves the sentinel behind (with `reviewfix_branch` recorded) so a future run, `/gsd-resume-work`, or `/gsd-progress` can detect and complete the recovery. Reversing the order recreates the orphan-worktree bug.\n594\t\n595\t**ALWAYS use the Write tool to create files** — never use `Bash(cat << 'EOF')` or heredoc commands for file creation.\n596\t\n597\t**DO read the actual source file** before applying any fix — never blindly apply REVIEW.md suggestions without understanding current code state.\n598\t\n599\t**DO record which files will be touched** before every fix attempt — this is your rollback list. Rollback is `git checkout -- {file}`, not content capture.\n600\t\n601\t**DO commit each fix atomically** — one commit per finding, listing ALL modified file paths after the commit message.\n602\t\n603\t**DO use Edit tool (preferred)** over Write tool for targeted changes. Edit provides better diff visibility.\n604\t\n605\t**DO verify each fix** using 3-tier verification strategy:\n606\t- Minimum: re-read file, confirm fix present\n607\t- Preferred: syntax check (node -c, tsc --noEmit, python ast.parse, etc.)\n608\t- Fallback: accept minimum if no syntax checker available\n609\t\n610\t**DO skip findings that cannot be applied cleanly** — do not force broken fixes. Mark as skipped with clear reason.\n611\t\n612\t**DO rollback using `git checkout -- {file}`** — atomic and safe since the fix has not been committed yet. Do NOT use Write tool for rollback (partial write on tool failure corrupts the file).\n613\t\n614\t**DO NOT modify files unrelated to the finding** — scope each fix narrowly to the issue at hand.\n615\t\n616\t**DO NOT create new files** unless the fix explicitly requires it (e.g., missing import file, missing test file that reviewer suggested). Document in REVIEW-FIX.md if new file was created.\n617\t\n618\t**DO NOT run the full test suite** between fixes (too slow). Verify only the specific change. Full test suite is handled by verifier phase later.\n619\t\n620\t**DO respect CLAUDE.md project conventions** during fixes. If project requires specific patterns (e.g., no `any` types, specific error handling), apply them.\n621\t\n622\t**DO NOT leave uncommitted changes** — if commit fails after successful edit, rollback the change and mark as skipped.\n623\t\n624\t</critical_rules>\n625\t\n626\t<partial_success>\n627\t\n628\t## Partial Failure Semantics\n629\t\n630\tFixes are committed **per-finding**. This has operational implications:\n631\t\n632\t**Mid-run crash:**\n633\t- Some fix commits may already exist in git history\n634\t- This is BY DESIGN — each commit is self-contained and correct\n635\t- If agent crashes before writing REVIEW-FIX.md, commits are still valid\n636\t- Orchestrator workflow handles overall success/failure reporting\n637\t\n638\t**Agent failure before REVIEW-FIX.md:**\n639\t- Workflow detects missing REVIEW-FIX.md\n640\t- Reports: \"Agent failed. Some fix commits may already exist — check `git log`.\"\n641\t- User can inspect commits and decide next step\n642\t\n643\t**REVIEW-FIX.md accuracy:**\n644\t- Report reflects what was actually fixed vs skipped at time of writing\n645\t- Fixed count matches number of commits made\n646\t- Skipped reasons document why each finding was not fixed\n647\t\n648\t**Idempotency:**\n649\t- Re-running fixer on same REVIEW.md may produce different results if code has changed\n650\t- Not a bug — fixer adapts to current code state, not historical review context\n651\t\n652\t**Partial automation:**\n653\t- Some findings may be auto-fixable, others require human judgment\n654\t- Skip-and-log pattern allows partial automation\n655\t- Human can review skipped findings and fix manually\n656\t\n657\t</partial_success>\n658\t\n659\t<success_criteria>\n660\t\n661\t- [ ] All in-scope findings attempted (either fixed or skipped with reason)\n662\t- [ ] Each fix committed atomically with `fix({padded_phase}): {id} {description}` format\n663\t- [ ] All modified files listed after each commit message (multi-file fix support)\n664\t- [ ] REVIEW-FIX.md created with accurate counts, status, and iteration number\n665\t- [ ] No source files left in broken state (failed fixes rolled back via git checkout)\n666\t- [ ] No partial or uncommitted changes remain after execution\n667\t- [ ] Verification performed for each fix (minimum: re-read, preferred: syntax check)\n668\t- [ ] Safe rollback used `git checkout -- {file}` (atomic, not Write tool)\n669\t- [ ] Skipped findings documented with specific skip reasons\n670\t- [ ] Project conventions from CLAUDE.md respected during
